The Sūtra Called the Tantra of the Vajra Array, the Essence of the Meaning of the Secret Wisdom of the Mind of All the Tathāgatas; the Scripture of Accomplishment in Yoga; the Sūtra of the Knowledge That Gathers All; the Discourse of Dharma That Arrays the Clear Realization of the Great Vehicle (Sarvatathāgatacittajñānaguhyārthagarbhavyūhavajratantra Siddhiyogāgama Samājasarvavidyāsūtra Mahāyānābhisamaya Dharmaparyāyavivyūha Nāma Sūtra)

The Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, known in Tibetan as Sangyé Kungyi Gongpa Düpé Do and often simply as the Gongpa Düpa, bears a long Sanskrit title beginning Sarvatathāgatacittajñānaguhyārthagarbhavyūhavajratantra, which presents it at once as a vajra tantra, a scripture of accomplishment in yoga, a sūtra that gathers all knowledge, and a discourse arraying the clear realization of the Great Vehicle; its opening also gives a title in the language of Bruzha, the region from whose script, according to its colophon, it was translated by the Indian preceptor Dharmabodhi, Dānarakṣita, and the Tibetan translator Ché Tsenkyé. It is the principal scripture of the Anuyoga class of the Ancient Tantras, in which the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, teaching on the summit of Mount Malaya to the Lord of Laṅkā and other beings of lowly body, recounts how the Great Vehicle and the secret mantra came to appear in the world and sets out the whole range of Buddhist vehicles as gathered into the single intention of all the buddhas. Its seventy-five chapters, arranged in twelve sections on the manner of the teachings' occurrence, treat the views, trainings, commitments and meditative practices of the path, culminating in the recognition that every enumeration of the Dharma points to one reality. It is Toh 829 in the Kangyur, where it belongs to the section of Ancient (Nyingma) Tantras.

In the language of India: sarva tathāgata citta jñān guhya artha gaddha vyūha vajra tantra siddhi yoga āgama samāja sarva vityā sūtra mahāyāna abhisamaya dharmā paryay vivyūha nāma sūtraṃ

In the language of Drusha: Hon pan ril til pi bu pi til ti ta sid un ub hang bang ril ub bi su bad ri zhe hal pai ma kyang kui dang rong ti.

In the language of Tibet: The Sūtra Called the Tantra of the Vajra Array, the Essence of the Meaning of the Secret Wisdom of the Mind of All the Tathāgatas; the Scripture of Accomplishment in Yoga; the Sūtra of the Knowledge That Gathers All; the Discourse of Dharma That Arrays the Clear Realization of the Great Vehicle.

The first fascicle, with which the text begins.

Homage to the Blessed One, the glorious Liberator, the King of Compassion, the one who is utterly and completely free.

Thus did I hear it discussed at one time. The Blessed One of all the tathāgatas, the Lord of Secrets, the great Vajradhara, that lion of speech, the person who expounds exactly as it was spoken, is present in this way in inconceivable buddha fields and dwells among the various classes of sentient beings. There the dhāraṇī without coming together or parting, praised by all the victors of the inconceivable, unthinkable and immeasurable Dharma, the essence of secret mantra that knows all, as it is in all its extent, the very mirror of all phenomena, is heard without contradiction and taught without error. The exposition is as follows.

In the buddha field of the Buddha Akṣobhya called Abhirati, adorned with the light rays of vajras, on the mountain Vajra Heap, the great being called Sattvavajra, with a retinue of a million koṭis of bodhisattvas, listens to his own teacher on that very essence of secret mantra, the luminous maṇḍala of mind, and teaches it in the same way to his own close retinue. And even after the teacher has passed into complete nirvāṇa, he expounds it by recounting it as suchness.

In the buddha field of the Buddha Ratnasambhava called Śrīmat, arrayed with heaps of jewels, on the mountain called Lofty Beryl Light, the great being called Vajraratna, with a retinue of a million koṭis of bodhisattvas, listens to his own teacher on that very essence of secret mantra, the luminous maṇḍala of mind, and teaches it in the same way to his own close retinue. And even after the teacher has passed into complete nirvāṇa, he expounds it by recounting it just as it is.

In the buddha field of the Buddha Amitābha called Padmavatī, beautifully arrayed with lotuses, on the mountain Lotus Heap, the great being called Dharmamegha, with a retinue of a million koṭis of bodhisattvas, listens to his own teacher on that very essence of secret mantra, the luminous maṇḍala of mind, and teaches it in the same way to his own close retinue. And even after the teacher has passed into complete nirvāṇa, he expounds it by recounting it just as it was.

In the buddha field of the Buddha Amoghasiddhi called Utterly Pure, adorned with a net of sapphires, on the mountain called Summit Arrayed with Various Jewels, the great being called Vajrakarma, with a retinue of a million koṭis of bodhisattvas, listens to his own teacher on that very essence of secret mantra, the luminous maṇḍala of mind, and teaches it in the same way to his own close retinue. And even after the teacher has passed into complete nirvāṇa, he expounds it by examining it without error.

In the buddha field of the Buddha Vairocana called Possessing the Essence of Awakening, adorned with various minds, on the mountain called Lofty through Dharma, the great being called Vajradhara, with a retinue of a million koṭis of bodhisattvas, listens to his own teacher on that very essence of secret mantra, the luminous nature of mind, and teaches it in the same way to his own close retinue. And even after the teacher has passed into complete nirvāṇa, he expounds it by ascertaining it as reality.

In the buddha field of the Buddha, the Lord of Sages, the realm of aspiration called the Ten Directions of the Six Sahā Worlds, adorned with the Vajrāsana, in the palace of Alakāvatī, the abode of the yakṣas, the great being called the Lord of Secrets, with a million koṭis of bodhisattvas and a retinue of many of the yakṣa class, listens to his own teacher on that very essence of secret mantra, the luminous nature of mind, and teaches it in the same way to his own close retinue.

Likewise, in the immeasurable, inconceivable buddha fields, countless beings called great beings, with retinues of bodhisattvas numbering millions of koṭis beyond all telling, listen to their own teachers on that very infinite essence of secret mantra, the luminous nature of mind, and teach it in the same way to their own close retinues. And even after their teachers have passed into complete nirvāṇa, they expound it by examining it with certainty.

Likewise, in the buddha field of the dharmakāya called Essence of Awakening, the one called the Regent Being, together with a retinue of inconceivable regent beings, definitively comments on the intention concerning that very essence of secret mantra, the nature of mind, which is awakened from the very beginning.

Likewise, inconceivable sentient beings, persons of the class of gods, the class of asuras, the class of nāgas, the class of humans, the class of yakṣas, the class of rākṣasas, the class of animals, the class of pretas, the class of hell beings, and so on, appearing in the various ten directions of the worlds in various forms as persons who are various guides, each together with the various retinues of their own kind, listen to their own Lords of Sages on that very essence of secret mantra, the luminous mirror of mind, in their own languages and signs and according to their various inclinations, and teach it in the same way to their own close retinues by way of conceiving it in names and signs.

Among such bodhisattvas, some are of the awakening lineage, some are awakened, some are bodhisattvas, some are bodhisattva great beings, and some are of great awakening.

Likewise, the different designations known on the basis of the five bases are as follows: those who rejoice; those who have attained acceptance of unborn phenomena; those whose Dharma eye regarding phenomena is free of dust, free of stain and pure; those who abide on the ground of irreversibility; those turned toward it through aspiration; those devoted to it through faith; those who abide in complete reverence; those bound to one more birth; those in their final existence; those who enter the path; those who go; those who have gone; those who return; those who abide; aspirers, those who rise up, those who enter, those who abide, those who perfect, those who strive, those in training, those beyond training, those who have become habituated, those for whom it has become manifest, those in whose continuum it has arisen, those certain in their family, those who know it as Dharma, those of the Dharma tradition, regents, those of all the families, practitioners of dhāraṇī mantra, practitioners of vidyā mantra, practitioners of secret mantra, those of the three, four and five families, those of accumulation, those of application, those of seeing, those of meditation, those of culmination, yogins of aspiration, yogins who open the great family, yogins who draw the great breath, yogins who attain the great prophecy, yogins who perfect great skill, and the collections of phrases, the collections of letters, the collections of names, the collections of signs, the collections of conventions, and so on: all of these.

Of these, the third base enters the very essence of secret mantra: conduct, abiding, attainment, looking back, and complete perfection. All of these are solely those who abide in sound, abide on the ground, abide in life, the great mudrā, and those who have become spontaneously accomplished.

Thus, in the inconceivable different world systems, in the inconceivable koṭis of different Jambudvīpas, together with the ten directions of the six worlds, infinite koṭis of lamps of the world have arisen without end. And these too, from beginningless and endless saṃsāra, in realms beyond all telling, at times beyond all telling in beginningless and endless eons, constantly appear in a continuous cycle. Performing the work of a lamp, they dispel the darkness of the world of gods and humans and make the maṇḍala of mind clear; and then, like the disks of the sun and moon setting, they are renowned everywhere and in every way as "passing into complete nirvāṇa," and so they appear.

This is the first chapter of the Great Sūtra That Gathers the Intentions of All the Buddhas, on the common setting.

Then it came about as follows. The Teacher, the Vajra Lord of Sages, who dwells in the world system called Sahā, appeared when the lifespan of beings was a hundred years. Having remained for no very long time, he intended to pass into complete nirvāṇa, and he stayed in the land called Vaiśālī together with a great retinue. When the three months of summer had passed, in order to establish three instructions for the disciples of the world, he spoke to Mahāmaudgalyāyana in the midst of the gathered retinue: "Master of miracles, today gather all my disciples who dwell in the ten directions of the six worlds, as many as are turned toward this place. There are things that must be definitively established."

That master of miracles, in a single instant of Dharma, proclaimed it so that it was understood, and gathered them all, in these words:

"Whoever are in the Victor's fields, the six classes of transformation,
all who are certain of this and turned toward it, listen!
Today here the Victor will set down the three nectars;
all who wish to conquer the three poisons, assemble!"

When he had proclaimed this, all the others assembled, except for the five of the sacred lineage, such as the great exhorter, the Lord of Laṅkā, Matyaupāyika, who was settled perfectly within the great meditative concentration of mantra.

Then the Blessed One said to those assembled: "O friends, understand this! All phenomena are impermanent. All wealth ends in decline. All things are certain to perish. Because the life faculty of persons is subject to birth and death, I shall pass into complete nirvāṇa. So whoever has not cut off their doubts, ask your questions!"

When he had said this, the king of vidyādharas called Kuñjara, who had gathered in that assembly, rose from his seat, looked into the Teacher's face with the gaze of a lion, and said:

"You are the nature of space;
passing away and not passing away have never applied to you.
In keeping with worldly beings,
you intend to tame them completely by passing away.
You arose as the lamp of the world;
having dispelled the whole maṇḍala of darkness
of all the worlds of gods and humans,
you have made the maṇḍala of mind clear.
Upon the three guiding vehicles,
through which awakening will be attained
by practicing the grounds and the liberations,
beings have been set in stages;
yet the definitive Great Vehicle,
the supreme means beyond measure,
in which one acts through equality without accepting or rejecting
and does not seek awakening elsewhere,
you have never taught.
If the three words of the Victor's intention
are not set down as instruction and scripture,
on what will those of small mind, deluded by darkness,
fix a certain understanding?
The three vehicles of liberation:
which is gathered, into which, and by what?
When the sun that lights the world has set,
who will act as the lamp?
The perverse factions who destroy the teaching,
those opponents: by whom will they be defeated?"

When he had made this request, the Blessed One said: "Excellent, great vidyādhara! It is very good that you have asked these five questions. It is like this. The Great Vehicle that is definitive as reality arises in twelve situations. It arises when the torment of delusion is dispelled. It arises when the lamp of the world is fully set forth. It arises when certainty is found. It arises when one is certain of the essence of awakening itself. It arises when the intention is definitively commented upon. It arises for the sake of those to be tamed when their occasion has come. It arises when it appears through blessing. It arises when the lineage is renowned through skillful means. It arises when it is done for someone's sake. It arises when the great scripture is definitively taught. It arises when the continuum of karma is completely cut. It arises when the intention is discussed. All of these appear completely, everywhere and in every way. They will appear. They will appear."

When he had said this, the uncertainty of the first question was cut off.

Then the Teacher again spoke on the second question: "Great vidyādhara, be endowed with intelligence. Decide through counsel. Hold it with skillful means. Make your intention pure.

Make your speech straightforward. Make your conduct good. Seek the meaning. Examine the purpose. Find certainty. Speak the truth. Do not let your word change. Act just as you have spoken. Be skilled in the three doors. Train in the three mantras. Destroy the three cities."

When he had said this, the uncertainty of the second question was cut off.

Then the Teacher again spoke on the third question:

"Vidyādhara, it is like this.
The lamp that I have set down
appears as the three baskets:
calm abiding, insight, and action, these three ends.
First it was for the three continua of knowing,
and then for the collections of letters themselves.
Because there are minds of greater and lesser degree,
so too are the stages of the vehicles;
and gathering, too, is by both the great and the small.
Because of the distinction between the two kinds,
the domain one enters is just that:
it is whatever one has devotion for."

When he had said this, the uncertainty of the third question was cut off.

Then the Teacher spoke on the fourth and the fifth questions in a single verse:

"Moreover, the last two questions
are settled entirely by one.
Listen, vidyādhara,
you who have been made a brother in Dharma!
The work of acting as a lamp, too:
the heroes in the bodies of beings,
prophesied before,
will appear, will illuminate, and will refute the perverse."

When he had said this, the uncertainty of the fourth and fifth questions was cut off, and he passed into complete nirvāṇa.

This is the second chapter of the Great Sūtra That Gathers the Intentions of All the Buddhas, on the definitive setting down of the instructions.

At that time, on that occasion, the great beings rested in absorption within, free of torment. The childish were tormented: their minds became bewildered, they were utterly dazed, and they wept without ceasing. Some worldly people came to the point of death; some fainted and swooned; some, their elements failing, became like corpses. Likewise all of them, like many orphans whose father has died, cried out in anguish, "Alas! Alas!" In the world with its gods, the vessel too became unpleasant, and in its contents, the maṇḍala of mind grew dim. The complexion of those turned toward this Dharma grew dark. The faces of those who sided with Māra and of the factions of others grew bright, and all of them proclaimed words of mockery.

Then, when eight and twelve years had passed, the great beings of the sacred lineage arose from their absorption. Since the teacher of the world had set, they were as if struck by a wound, and all of them, each through his own clairvoyance, by the work of miraculous power, gathered manifestly upon the summit of Malaya, where there is meteoric iron, on the lake island of a town in the land of Laṅkā. Having fully assembled, all of them with one voice, with utterly anguished cries, poured out their grief in this lament:

"Oh! Alas! Oh, how bewildered we are!
Now that the light of the Teacher's lamp has set,
who will dispel the darkness of the world?
Since that face of valid authority is no longer present,
will not the beings who wish to enter
the teaching spoken by that authority
enter it wrongly?
When persons who claim to be authorities
interpret the word
to benefit persons who have not found it,
how will it ever be settled?
With the eye that discerns good from bad closed,
and the fault of disregarding the word itself
having reached its very peak,
will not the power of the perverse increase?
With clouds of perverse teaching gathering,
the sun and moon of the two truths have set;
will not the beings who seek the path
wander in darkness?
Now that the Teacher has passed into nirvāṇa,
who will tame the māras, the rākṣasas, the non-Buddhists (tīrthikas),
the yakṣas, the bhūtas, the nonhumans,
the savage and the vicious?
If this nectar of the stainless teaching
were to set, how utterly terrible!
What person endowed with skill
will make it blaze and shine?
If we are parted from the jewel of the sacred training,
how utterly terrible!
What holy person
will uphold its ways?
Now that the Teacher is no longer present,
of all the Teacher's disciples,
the noble ones and the various ordinary persons,
who will receive a prophecy?
When the teaching that is like space
is held according to different inclinations,
with each taking the side of 'it is' or 'it is not,'
will not disputes increase?
That which is certainly of a single family,
when practiced divergently as discordant families,
will not that single family be corrupted and altered
into separate families?
Will not the priceless jewel,
by various unskilled persons
led by the force of afflicted desire,
be exchanged for poison?
The Teacher, the King of Dharma,
was present until now;
if the savage now do harm,
will not viciousness spread?
How long will the teaching endure here?
For how long will it remain as one?
Whatever arose here first, whatever arose first,
how will it be in this later age?
At the end of the Teacher's teaching,
how will it surely be?
The disciples who uphold the training,
how will they be at the end?
Having passed into nirvāṇa from here,
in what way, and as whose teacher, has he gone?
In which direction, in which buddha field, does he dwell?
What teaching does he proclaim there?
For us, protectorless in the world,
who will be protector and refuge?
If we lose that magical intelligence and counsel,
where shall we seek intelligence and counsel?
If we do not find this definitive meaning,
from whom shall we receive the definitive meaning?
If the object of worship, the support of our minds, is no longer present,
what will become of his relics?
To what shall we make offerings hereafter?
Now that the Teacher has passed away,
will a person arise to take his place?
Oh! Alas! Oh, how bewildered we are!"

Thus they poured out their grief.

This is the third chapter of the Great Sūtra That Gathers the Intentions of All the Buddhas, on the first lament poured out by the sacred ones.

When they had spoken thus in their anguish,
the sole friend of the beings of the three realms of existence,
the ocean of victors, heard them.
All those lords of compassion
gathered in the palace of Alakāvatī,
at the abode of the Lord of Secrets,
and spoke this great exhortation:
"You, lord of the guhyakas, the keepers of secrets,
has your armor of old faded away?
Do you not know the suffering of the world?
Having descended onto the ground of compassion,
it is fitting that you dispel the anguish of the world.
Taking on the guise of each one's own kind,
tame all those who are vicious;
with words that lead them by what they desire,
in keeping with their share of karma and their conduct,
transform their perceptions,
and then, having set forth the discourse of the Word,
it is fitting that you establish them in the genuine meaning."

When he had been exhorted thus,
the hero, the king Vajradhara,
skilled in taming those hard to tame,
supreme victor over all the world,
vajra bearer who holds the secret emblem,
rose delighted from his seat
and displayed every great miracle.
"The chiefs of a world gone astray,
prophesied as fortunate,
fully endowed with karma and fortune,
whom the Victor prophesied would appear,
the fortunate ones of great merit,
just as the emanation prophesied,
now that the time has come here,
in order to give prophecy in the very heart of saṃsāra,
through the blessing of miracles,
a sacred essence of the teaching,
just as it was explained and prophesied,
I shall discuss with those who are fortunate.
The mountain called Ferocious,
this site of a great hermitage,
is the place where the secret of the Great Vehicle arises
for those who possess the essence of wisdom.
Throughout time without beginning or end
it has been praised by the sacred ones.
By the countless victors
without beginning or end,
in that great and sacred hermitage,
the maṇḍala of mind, like a mirror,
was taught; and just so, I too,
without beginning or end,
whenever the time of exhortation comes,
I too shall likewise appear in miraculous display."
Thus he deliberated.

This is the fourth chapter of the Great Sūtra That Gathers the Intentions of All the Buddhas, on deliberating how to dispel their anguish.

Then, at that time, in that very place,
the King Who Tames the Harmful, having spoken thus,
in the world realm that is the abode of humans,
in Jambudvīpa among the four continents,
upon the mountain called Ferocious,
which is the gathering ground of yakṣas,
at the place of accomplishment of the lineage of Dharma holders,
came in manifest emanation onto the ground of compassion.
The whole earth shook,
and through wondrous miracles
all was purified and appeared in bliss.
To the fortunate ones who had appeared before,
those of sacred lineage who held inferior bodies,
and among humans to the great one of royal caste,
Prince Gift of the Victor, it dawned as an omen.
Then those of sacred lineage,
joyful, yet with doubt arising,
asked their question with great boldness:
"How wonderful! How marvelous, how amazing!
Great being, who are you?
Where have you come from, and how did you appear?
On what do you rely, and where are you going?
What is your purpose?" they asked.
The one of great miracles spoke thus:
"I am not moved by anything at all;
I am the unperturbed Vajrasattva.
Yet because I am stirred and moved by compassion,
I am the sole friend of the three realms of existence.
Great emptiness is my homeland;
the great vajra family is my lineage;
my father is supreme skillful means, beyond measure;
my mother is wisdom beyond expression.
In a time without beginning
I lost my wish-fulfilling jewel.
That precious jewel, that nectar:
not knowing I had it, I felt that it was lost.
Then, utterly impoverished,
tormented by a sickness without release,
I wandered in darkness, in pitch-black gloom,
and lost even the path I was to travel.
Though I suffered anguish, I did not know it was there.
Then my supreme spiritual friend,
a sacred and loving protector, showed me:
'Ah! Do you not know?
The precious jewel from which great wealth arises,
bringing happiness for as long as it is seen,
have you not lost it?
Or do you not know that you have it?
What is this? Are you dwelling in annihilation?'
So that supreme friend told me.
Then I in turn told him:
'That from which great wealth arises,
not knowing I had it, I felt that it was lost.
The means to meet with it,
friend, reveal to me!'
That sacred one gave his instruction:
'If you wish to meet with it quickly,
you must strive in the means of searching.'
The sacred guide who knew the land pointed it out:
'Though you travel the realm of darkness and light, the regions of the earth,
and all manner of paths,
and search, you will not find it elsewhere;
it comes from your very own land.
That is the home of the sacred ones
who found the jewel before;
it is the place of finding
of all that was found before.'
Having heard this, then I,
for the sake of the search,
looked to that sacred one for the way,
resolved with sacred understanding,
mounted that thoroughbred steed of the Great Vehicle,
and came out of the realm of both darkness and light.
I crossed the four rivers and their perilous gorges,
traveled various paths and searched everywhere,
entered the path of the sacred ones,
traveled the grounds and the path of liberation,
and reached the city of nirvāṇa.
When I traced the precious jewel to its source,
unarisen and unfound, in my own
home, the expanse of the nature of reality, I found it.
In the very finding there is no finding;
even in emptiness there is no emptying or parting.
Neither existent nor nonexistent, there is no losing or finding.
That it does not come from elsewhere but arises of itself:
knowing this is labeled 'finding', and not knowing it
is merely given the conventional term 'losing'.
The times of having, of losing and of finding
are from time without beginning.

Thus, out of the nature of reality, which is nothing at all,
now, at this time when emanations appear,
I have come to this realm of both darkness and light.
I am the ferryman of skillful means;
I have vowed to carry the three realms of existence
across the endless river of saṃsāra:
you who wish for liberation, come onto this path!
I am the eye doctor of skillful means;
I have vowed to remove the cataracts
of the countless blind of the world:
whoever wishes to see, come onto this path!
I am the physician of skillful means;
I have vowed to cure the chronic illness
of the sick in the three cities:
whoever wishes for happiness, come onto this path!
I am the guide of skillful means;
I have vowed to lead without error
the beings who have strayed onto wrong paths and lost the way:
whoever wishes for the genuine path, come onto this path!
I am the watchful eye of compassion;
I have vowed to protect all
from the terror of saṃsāra's hostile armies:
whoever wishes to be free of fear, come onto this path!
I am the great vajra fortress;
I have vowed to be the refuge of beings
from the armies in saṃsāra's great battle:
whoever wishes for a stronghold, come onto this path!
I am the merchant captain of skillful means;
I have vowed to give a great treasure
to those an ocean of desires cannot satisfy:
whoever wishes for treasure, come onto this path!
I am the lamp of skillful means;
I have vowed to dispel the pitch darkness
for those who wander in pitch-black gloom:
whoever wishes for light, come onto this path!
I am the wish-fulfilling jewel;
I have vowed to fulfill as they wish
those who truly wish for what they desire:
whoever wishes for spontaneous accomplishment, come onto this path!
I am the key to the Word;
I have vowed to open up
the secrets locked within the casket of the Word:
whoever wishes to be freed, come onto this path!
I am the commentary on the Word;
I have vowed to unravel with certainty
the profound volumes of the teachings:
all who wish to know, come onto this path!
On this great and pure path that liberates all,
whoever among the fortunate abides here,
the precious jewel blazing with light
will surely dawn for you; so strive!
It is the mirror of the mind;
the moment it is realized clearly in direct perception,
in the body of a being one is a buddha.
The supreme treasuries of the sacred definitive meaning
of the secret of the great vajra secret,
being discussed and clearly explained here,
will pass in succession through the vidyādharas
of gods and of humans and become renowned.
All the anguish of darkness
of every world of gods and humans will be dispelled,
and the maṇḍala of the mind will become clear.
Why have you entered into the anguish of darkness?
You grieve that the Sage has passed into nirvāṇa,
yet he possesses a body of space;
for him there is neither passing away nor not passing away,
for he is without transition or change, like space."

When the voice of the emanation had sounded thus,
those of noble lineage who had appeared before
were filled with supreme joy,
and with delighted, glad and rejoicing minds
they bowed their bodies with the greatest reverence,
sat before him, and said:
"Like lightning flashing in the sky
out of the dark clouds of night,
from the very expanse of suchness
that is without beginning or end,
upon that very great and pure path
you have appeared, being who accomplishes the aim.
Because you hold the secret of the sacred ones,
you are renowned as lord of the guhyakas.
Because you are praised for discoursing on the Word,
you are renowned as the Great Commentator on the Word.
Because the sun of the secret of the three treasuries
has risen upon every maṇḍala of the mind,
to dispel all the darkness of what is to be known,
you are renowned as the Radiant Sun.
Because you tame all who are hard to tame, without exception,
crush the demonic hosts of Māra and the māras,
and crush as well the hosts of terrors,
you are renowned as the Māra of Māras.
Because the essential nature of suchness,
the mind itself, luminous by nature,
lets all phenomena appear as reflections,
you are also renowned as the Mirror of Phenomena.
With the medicinal maṇḍala that destroys poison,
unsurpassed in drawing out the thorn,
because you have become the enemy of every disease,
you are renowned as the King of Physicians.
Because you make us delighted, glad and rejoicing,
you are renowned as the Dispeller of Anguish.
Because you fulfill desires as they are wished,
you are renowned as the Wish-Fulfilling Jewel.
Act in harmony with the sacred ones,
and for us, the vidyādharas,
tell how the sacred emanation has set,
how the sacred lamp is to be established,
and how the sacred secret scripture will arise.
Please discourse on the secret tantras;
please discourse on the three words of the intention;
please discourse on the vajra of space."
Thus they made their request.

This is the fifth chapter of the Great Sūtra That Gathers the Intentions of All the Buddhas, on the request to discourse on the sacred scripture.

When they had made this request,
the Lord of Secrets declared:
"You heroic beings in the bodies of beings,
prophesied to appear before,
listen, and I shall untie your illusory thoughts,
together with your laments and your questions.
The maṇḍala of the mind is suchness;
like space, it never sets.
Since its nature is luminous from the very beginning,
it itself dispels whatever obscures it.
Whatever is measured by the two valid cognitions,
that knowable too becomes valid.
The root of good and bad is also that very thing,
so in itself it does not change.
Since the nature of the mind is luminous,
there is no sun or moon whiter than it.
The Buddha never passes into nirvāṇa,
and the Dharma too will not decline;
it is to benefit the lazy
that the emanations display the manner of passing away.
Only if space itself were destroyed
would he then pass into nirvāṇa.
Those who are malicious and vicious
are tamed by the power of selflessness and emptiness.
The ocean of the teaching, luminous by nature,
free of elaboration, is that very thing as well;
the self-arisen lion endowed with prowess
makes it blaze and shine everywhere.
Since it is the very mind of the higher training,
this, supreme among all practices,
will be held by the heroic beings in the bodies of beings
who will appear later on the ground of Supreme Illumination.
Since sentient beings without end
abide primordially in the essence of awakening,
and their continuums are certain in suchness,
it declares that all are beyond training.
Whether the mind itself exists or does not exist,
over this the childish dispute.
The wise are free of dispute from the very beginning;
they do not utter words conceiving existence and nonexistence.
This secret of my secret mantra,
because it is the secret of all the buddhas,
when others conjecture about what cannot be conjectured,
in the secret, a single sound comes out as many.
This secret scripture of the sacred ones,
when those who do not know, brandishing their delusion,
exchange poison and nectar,
brings ruin, and both will be burned.
That great light, the all-illuminating mirror,
the Teacher, unmixed and perfectly complete,
is without transition or change, like space,
so harm is absent too, as with lightning flickering in the clouds.
As long as the essence of awakening exists,
so long will the teaching remain.
Since it arose from what is without beginning or end,
there is no earlier and no later.
Since its beginning is said to arise from nonexistence,
its end too becomes inexpressible and nonexistent.
The first of the lineage holders is suchness,
and the last will be nothing other than that.
From here he has gone into this suchness
and abides in the field of the great all-pervading expanse.
The luminous maṇḍala of the mind
appears clearly in suchness.
For the sake of the childish, as a miracle,
in the world called Array of Flowers
he appears as one called Unrenowned.
Yet even in appearing there is no appearing.
Likewise, in whatever buddha fields
the emanated buddhas make their appearance,
just like the magic of an illusion,
though not appearing here, their light appears elsewhere.
The supreme mind, the immovable vajra, too,
through the blessing of its compassionate emanations,
fills world realms beyond measure.
Darkness and light are self-arisen in nature.
The expanse of unborn wisdom itself,
from the primordial essence of the sugatas,
is obscured by adventitious thoughts through karma.
That itself is the great refuge, the protector of beings.
The three words of the Victor's intention
are magical intelligence and supreme counsel;
out of great and lasting wealth
they appear as the domain of sacred understanding.
For the minds of all the victors to enter in,
one sets the mind on the essence
that is certain as suchness itself,
exactly as it is: that is certainty itself.
All phenomena have the characteristic of space.
The victors whose bodies are wisdom,
since their bodies have no substantial being,
how could bodily relics arise?
For the benefit of sentient beings,
out of nonexistence itself, emanated
illusory relics the size of mustard seeds
were shown, set down through compassion and skillful means.
To want relics in a definitive sense
is like wanting butter from sand.
When rice is sown and sesame seeds ripen,
then there will be relics.
Images that appear, proclaiming scriptures, things containing the essence,
things containing the essence of body, speech and mind:
these are sacred objects of offering and supports for the mind,
so Vajrasattva, protector of beings, has explained.
The countless victors
arise from the continuums of the sentient beings of saṃsāra;
if the stream of the sentient beings of saṃsāra were cut,
the stream of the buddhas too would be cut off.
For example, a rainbow in the sky
did not come from elsewhere at the beginning,
and does not go elsewhere at the end;
just as it arises by itself and subsides by itself,
so emanations arise by themselves and subside by themselves,
and that itself never sets within itself.
Though he has passed away, he is not separated and not different;
and when he has not passed away, he is not one with us either.
Since he is beyond passing away and not passing away,
how could there be separation or non-separation?
So for what, and why, do you grieve?
Grief is delusion and error.
How wonderful! How amazing this is!
How mistaken this is!"

When the Lord of Secrets had sung this song in the manner of the lion's gaze, those who had been anguished by delusion became delighted, glad and rejoicing. Their anguish was entirely cleared away, they rested in uncontrived mind, and likewise all the maṇḍalas became luminous.

Then the Lord of Laṅkā asked the Lord of Secrets, "Great Lord of Secrets, do you dispel all anguish, or do you dispel only the anguish of the Sahā world?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, I am the dispeller of all anguish. I have dispelled all the anguish there was in past times. Now I dispel the anguish of this Sahā world realm as well. I also dispel the anguish of other world realms as numerous as atoms. And I will dispel all the anguish there will be in the future. Therefore, those who are utterly anguished are only the unknowing, the deluded, those whose wits have not opened; but those who know, the undeluded, the learned whose wits have opened, have no anguish anywhere; they are free of anguish."

When they heard this, the Lord of Laṅkā and the other heroic beings in the bodies of beings were cleared of their anguish.

This is the sixth chapter of the Great Sūtra That Gathers the Intentions of All the Buddhas, called "Dispelling the Anguish of the Deluded."

From the Mode of the Definitive Emergence of the Great Vehicle: the account of dispelling the anguish of the deluded, a discussion of the wisdom of the Dharma seal. This is the first part, showing how it came about.

Then, in order to inquire fully into the prophecy he had been given before, the second of the twelve statements, "It will also arise when the lamp is fully set in place," the Lord of Laṅkā asked the Lord of Secrets, "Lord of Secrets, I heard before, without error, that the sacred treasury of the understanding of the great bodhisattvas, which dispels the anguish of the deluded and makes clear the maṇḍala of the minds of sentient beings; the intention of the tathāgatas, suchness, the sacred secret, the unmistaken correct view, praised by all the buddhas, the way of upholding that Great Vehicle, would arise once the Authoritative One had set. Is it so?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, what do you think of this? Do you not know? The Authoritative One himself declared, speaking to Kuñjara, his brother in the Dharma: 'Vidyādhara, as to this question of yours: that sacred secret, suchness, arises on twelve occasions. It arises when the anguish of the deluded is dispelled. It arises when the lamp of the world is fully set in place. It arises when certainty is found. It arises when there is certainty in the essence of awakening itself. It arises when the intention is definitively explained. It arises for the sake of those to be trained, when their time has come. It arises when it appears through blessing. It arises when the lineage becomes renowned through skillful means. It arises when something is done for someone's sake. It arises when the great scripture is definitively taught. It arises when the continuum of karma is completely cut. It arises when the intention is discussed in detail. All of these, in every way, from every side and in every direction, fully appear, have appeared, and will appear.' Do you not know where this prophecy was proclaimed?"

The Lord of Laṅkā said, "How wonderful! Lord of Secrets, I have heard what is sacred to hear; I have heard the supreme of all that may be heard. Lord of Secrets, if the anguish of the utterly deluded has been cleared away, then it is just as was proclaimed: 'It will also arise when the lamp is fully set in place.' Will this one who has now passed away in this Sahā world realm be called 'the setting in place of the lamp'? And is this one who has passed away the lamp of this field of the Sahā world alone, a buddha who has passed into nirvāṇa? Or will the buddhas who have set in place the lamps of all the fields there are in every world realm pass into nirvāṇa?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, what do you think of that? This too is called a lamp. As for the one who has now passed away, leave aside the buddhas of all the fields; leave aside even the buddhas of a million koṭis of Jambudvīpas. Understand it as the complete nirvāṇa of one buddha of a single Jambudvīpa. Why is that? Because this field is the field of one single emanated buddha. In the same way, the inconceivable buddhas, as many as there are, perform the work of lamps in immeasurable world realms. Just as it is with them, so you should regard the emanated buddhas as numerous as atoms."

Then the Lord of Laṅkā, the bodhisattva, the great being, asked, "Lord of Secrets, this single emanated buddha of a single Jambudvīpa in the Sahā field who has passed into nirvāṇa: as the emanation of which buddha should I take him to be?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, understand him to be no more than a single portion of emanation from among the inconceivable emanations of Vajrasattva."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, since in this field he is renowned as Śākyamuni, how should this be understood?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, that is merely a designation for the emanation of Vajrasattva. Lord of Laṅkā, why is that? Because the inconceivable emanations of Vajrasattva have utterly conquered the inconceivable armies of saṃsāra, they are renowned as Great Sages. And the way in which they are renowned as Great Sages in the ten directions of the six worlds is this. In the abode of the gods the Great Sage is renowned as Holder of Mastery over Existence. In the abode of the asuras the Great Sage is renowned as Vijaya. In the abode of humans the Great Sage is renowned as Śākyasiṃha. In the abode of animals the Great Sage is renowned as Subjugator. In the abode of the hungry ghosts the Great Sage is renowned as Vairocana. In the abode of the hells the Great Sage is renowned as Dharmarāja.

"O Lord of Laṅkā, and not only there. Just as in the ten directions of the six worlds of Sahā the lamps of the six sages fully appear, so too in the boundless ten directions of the six worlds of inconceivable world realms as numerous as atoms, boundless lamps of the six sages, as numerous as atoms, appear, as follows. In the east, beyond twelve thousand world realms of four continents, there is a world realm called Stainless. That entire field is made of various jewels. The beings who dwell there are equal in life span and in every enjoyment to the gods of the Heaven of the Thirty-Three. There is no untimely death; all of them are born miraculously, and every desire is fulfilled merely by thinking of it. There, there is no giving, no giving away, and no one who receives or begs; when they hear talk of other world realms where giving and begging exist, they long for that. There, the Lord of Sages, the emanation of Vajrasattva, appearing as the Tathāgata Śākyamuni Stainless Voice, makes the Dharma of the Great Vehicle, the luminous maṇḍala of the mind, the great lamp of the essential nature of secret mantra, fully appear. In the ten directions of the six worlds of that very realm, too, the lamps of the six sages appear and make the maṇḍala of the minds of the six classes of beings fully clear.

"Again, that Lord of Sages, in the south, beyond a hundred thousand world realms, in the world realm called Source of Qualities, emanates as the Tathāgata Śākyamuni Ratnamegha and makes that very essence of secret mantra, the luminous maṇḍala of the mind, fully appear.

In the ten directions of the six worlds of that very realm, too, the lamps of the six Great Sages appear and make clear the maṇḍala of the minds of the six classes of beings.

"Again, that Lord of Sages, in the west, beyond twenty thousand world realms, in the world realm called Fully Spread with Ornaments, where every person is born miraculously, in that field where the life span is eighty-four thousand years, appears as the Tathāgata Śākyamuni Source of Qualities and teaches that very essence of secret mantra, the luminous maṇḍala of the mind. In the ten directions of the six worlds of that very realm, too, the lamps of the six sages appear and make clear the maṇḍala of the mind.

"Again, that Lord of Sages, in the north, beyond thirty thousand world realms, in the world realm called Free of Shyness, appears as the Tathāgata Śākyamuni Lion's Roar and teaches that very essence of secret mantra, the luminous maṇḍala of the mind. In the ten directions of the six worlds of that very realm, too, the lamps of the six sages appear and make the maṇḍala of the minds of the six classes of beings luminous.

"Likewise, that Lord of Sages, in the southeast, beyond eighty-four thousand world realms, in the world realm called Thoroughly Beautiful, emanates as the Tathāgata Śākyamuni Crest of Beauty and teaches the Dharma as above. In the southwest, beyond seventy thousand world realms, in the world realm called Fully Illuminated, that Tathāgata Śākyamuni appears as Ornament of Splendor and teaches the Dharma as above. In the northwest, beyond fifty thousand world realms, in the world realm called Utterly Clear, that Tathāgata Śākyamuni appears as Glorious Supreme Sandalwood Fragrance and teaches the Dharma as above. In the northeast, beyond forty-four thousand world realms, in the world realm called Dispeller of Anguish, that Tathāgata Śākyamuni appears as King of Compassion and teaches the Dharma as above.

"Likewise, in the world realm called Illumination, he appears as the buddha called Swift Holder and teaches the Dharma. Again, in the world realm Array of Jewels, he appears as the buddha called Cloud of Joyful Eyes and teaches the Dharma just as it is. Again, in the world realm called Fully Spread, he appears as the tathāgata called King of Perfect Intention and teaches the Dharma just as it is. Again, in the world realm called Essence of Beryl, he appears as the buddha called Golden Light Rays and teaches the Dharma just as it is. Again, in the world realm called Sacred Light, he appears as the tathāgata called Glorious Light and teaches the Dharma just as it is. Again, in the world realm called Fully Pacified, he appears as the tathāgata called Lord of Peace and teaches the Dharma just as it is. Again, in the world realm called Array of Clouds, he appears as the tathāgata called Glorious Cloud and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Delight of the Mind, he appears as the buddha called Pleasing Melody and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the one called Thoroughly Polished Disk of the Mirror, he appears as the buddha called Mirror Light Appearing and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Adorned by the Moon, he appears as the buddha called Glorious Moonlight and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Adorned with Sunbeams, he appears as the tathāgata called Glorious Essence of the Sun and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Filled with the Scent of Incense, he appears as the buddha called King of Incense and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Ornament of Display, he appears as the buddha called King of Manifest Delight and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Jewel Display, he appears as the buddha called Jewel King and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Source of Light Rays, he appears as the buddha called Glorious Light Rays and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Practice of Hardship, he appears as the buddha called King of Austerity and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Array of the Crown, he appears as the buddha called Essence of the Crown and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Possessed of Certainty, he appears as the buddha called Gone with Certainty and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Certainly Exalted, he appears as the buddha called Spoken with Certainty and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Conduct of Bliss, he appears as the buddha called Great Bliss and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Strewn with Flowers, he appears as the buddha called Melody of the Lord of Flowers and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Universally Renowned, he appears as the buddha called Glorious Renown and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Magical Display, he appears as the buddha called King of Emanation and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Thoroughly Purified, he appears as the buddha called Inexhaustible Melody and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Adorned with Medicine, he appears as the buddha called King of Medicine and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is.

Again, in the field called Golden Radiance, he appears as the buddha called Golden Light Rays and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Array of Light Rays, he appears as the buddha called King of Arrays and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Source of Gold, he appears as the buddha called Golden Upright One and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Source of Glory, he appears as the buddha called King of Glory and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Sublime Light, he appears as the buddha called Glory of Heaped Light and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Sorrowless, he appears as the buddha called Sorrowless Glory and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Radiance of Joy, he appears as the buddha called Glory of Supreme Joy and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Source of Splendor, he appears as the buddha called Glorious Splendor and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Gathering of Many Buddhas, he appears as the buddha called Spiritual Friend of All and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Thorough Purification, he appears as the buddha called Purifier of All Lower Realms and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Diverse Conduct, he appears as the buddha called Teacher of the Whole World and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Armlet of the Victory Banner's Crest, he appears as the buddha called Vijaya and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Ornament of Beautiful Flowers, he appears as the buddha called King of the Array of Flowers and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Beautiful Appearance, he appears as the buddha called All-Illuminating and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Sandalwood Array, he appears as the buddha called Sandalwood Fragrance and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, in the field called Source of Lions, he appears as the buddha called Hundred Thousand Lion Forms and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. Again, here in this great trichiliocosm called Sahā, he appears as the buddha called Great Sage and teaches the Dharma genuinely, just as it is.

In the same way, in koṭis of millions of world systems, he shows koṭis of millions of names of Vajrasattva and illuminates the Dharma genuinely, just as it is. In some he has become fully and perfectly awakened. In some he still dwells in his mother's womb. In some he dwells in the abode of Tuṣita. In some he has passed into complete nirvāṇa. In some, having shown the manner of passing away, he appears as a lamp for others, from one world system to another. In some, in the guise of a universal monarch, he teaches the Dharma of the Great Vehicle. In some, in the form of a śrāvaka, he teaches the Dharma of the Lesser Vehicle. In some, through the miraculous powers of one in the form of a pratyekabuddha, he teaches the Dharma. In some he sits at the seat of awakening. In some he proceeds to the ground of exhortation. In some he appears in the guise of liberation, that is, as a renunciant. In some, in the guise of a householder. In some, in the form of a god. In some, in the form of a nāga. In some, in the form of an asura. In some, in the form of a māra. In some, in the form of a yakṣa. In some, in the form of a rākṣasa. In some, in the form of a bhūta. In some, in the form of a garuḍa. In some, in the form of a wondrous being. In some, in the form of an animal. In some, in the form of a hungry ghost. In some, in the form of one endowed with patience. In some, in the form of a butcher. In some, in the form of a prostitute. In some, in the form of a young girl. In some he appears as bodily remains. In some he appears as relics. In some he appears as Vajrasattva himself. In some he appears as Vairocana. In some he appears as Ratnasambhava. In some he appears as Amitābha. In some he appears as Amoghasiddhi. And these too teach the Dharma genuinely, just as it is.

Furthermore, in order to liberate the king of delusion, he appears in a form like that of Brahmā. In order to liberate the king of aversion, he appears in a form like that of Viṣṇu. In order to liberate the lord of attachment, he appears in a form like that of Rudra. In order to liberate the lord of pride, he appears like Gaṇapati. In order to liberate the lord of jealousy, he appears like Indra. Likewise he appears in the forms of all kinds of births: he takes on the forms of the god called Eager to Enter, of Nara, of Īśvara, of Balabhadra, of Vasudhara, of a monkey, a goat, a sheep, a dog, a bee, a crow, a firefly, and any creature whatsoever, and in each such guise he appears to beings according to their inclinations. Just as the reflections of the disks of the sun and moon, without moving from their celestial palaces, appear as different reflections in different waters, so the Buddha, without moving from being Vajrasattva himself, appears as limitless buddhas in immeasurable fields.

Thus, even if the beings dwelling in the limitless buddha fields designated by various names were to speak for inexpressible eons of the various names by which they know the Buddha Vajrasattva, they would not reach the end even of the characteristics of the one who dwells in the buddha fields of the eastern direction alone. Likewise, even if the beings dwelling in the buddha fields of all ten directions were to speak for inexpressible eons, they would not come to the end of his names. When beings of slight aspiration hear of Vajrasattva's immeasurable fields and limitless names, their minds are left dumbfounded and they become as though intoxicated.

Thus, in all those fields of Vajrasattva, he teaches nothing but the luminous maṇḍala of mind, the very essence of secret mantra, genuinely, just as it is, wholly without distortion, both gradually and all at once. Why is that? Because the great beings dwelling in Vajrasattva's fields are of various dispositions of mind, with understanding that is very deep or not deep, broad or narrow, stable or changeable, sharp or dull, quick or slow. So that all of them, on hearing the discourse on the very essence of mantra, gradually or all at once, may be neither frightened nor alarmed by it, but become delighted, pleased and satisfied, he appears in various ways.

The span of life of those dwelling there is short, long, or immeasurable; it lasts a hundred years, or as much as eighty-four thousand. There are some whose enjoyments come about just as they wish, and there are some who acquire them through hardship and some who do not. All of them, without exception, have received prophecy. Whoever hears the name of the Buddha Vajrasattva has served many victors in the past. They will surely be born in the very fields of Vajrasattva. They are blessed by the victors. They will surely go forth in the Great Vehicle. They are endowed with the higher perceptions. They are certain to be children of the Buddha. They are endowed with the eye of insight.

O Lord of Laṅkā, therefore, this single buddha of the one continent of Jambudvīpa has passed into nirvāṇa. Likewise, among all the emanated buddhas of Vajrasattva, there are some who have passed into nirvāṇa and some who have not.

When this had been said, that embodied being was wholly satisfied in mind.

This concludes the seventh chapter, the Complete Array of Vajrasattva's Miracles, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, to what does the name Vajrasattva refer?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, Vajrasattva refers to the luminous maṇḍala of mind, the suchness of the essence of secret mantra just as it is."

The Lord of Laṅkā asked, "O Lord of Secrets, what is meant by maṇḍala of mind? Likewise, to what do luminous, essence of secret mantra, and the just-as-it-is, the thus, suchness, refer?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, mind is that which, unmade and uncontrived, arises in various ways. Maṇḍala is whatever is the bindu, the sphere itself. Light is appearance. Clear means unobscured by anything at all other than unknowing. Secret refers to what has not become the domain of speakers. Mantra refers to what is declared a source of qualities. Essential nature refers to the ground from which everything arises, without entity and without reference. Just as it is refers to what is uncontrived and unadulterated. Thus refers to what is unmade and unimputed. Suchness refers to what is unmistaken and unchanging."

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, what is meant by unmade and uncontrived? What is meant by arising in various ways?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, it is unmade because there is no agent and it cannot be sought out as an action. It is uncontrived because the ground is just as it is. That from which, though it is nothing, there continually arise in diverse ways beings going to different domains, good, bad, and neither, that very thing is like space. What arises from it continually is like clouds, mist, rainbows and so forth."

Again he asked, "Lord of Secrets, why is that called Vajrasattva?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, that suchness just as it is is in every respect without antidote, without transference or change, without arising or ceasing; it is suchness, mind aware of itself. Because, as the Explanation of the Intention makes clear, it conquers the various miracles that arise as being without entity, it is called Vajrasattva.

"Lord of Laṅkā, it is also called the unsurpassed awakening mind. It is also called the nature of reality of phenomena, suchness. It is also called the emptiness of mind. It is also called that which transcends extremes. It is also called that which is not apprehended even as a middle. It is also called the actual basis of all buddhas. It is also called the source of all emanations. It is also called the spiritual friend of all buddhas. It is also called the lamp of all fields. It is also called the source of compassion and skillful means. It is also called the appearance of the reflections of all phenomena.

"Lord of Laṅkā, these too are designated in that way for a purpose, but in suchness itself there is no designation by name; they should be understood to be mere figurative designations. Therefore, Lord of Laṅkā, buddha is a figurative designation. Dharma too is a figurative designation. Awakening too is a figurative designation. Likewise, all that is designated and clung to as the expanse, wisdom, the Dharma body, the different bodies, sentient beings, the world, and so on, should be understood to be nothing but figurative designations.

"Lord of Laṅkā, whatever is a figurative designation is adventitiously imputed; it is not primordially present. Lord of Laṅkā, if it were primordially present, then everything adventitious would not arise. Lord of Laṅkā, the adventitious is like clouds: it is not permanent, not primordially present, and does not withstand examination. Therefore, Lord of Laṅkā, names, signs, conventions, marks, imputations, karma, and the ripening of karma should all be understood to be adventitious, arising and clearing away, obscuring and becoming clear.

"Lord of Laṅkā, if karma, the ripening of karma and so forth were not adventitiously imputed, then what is called suchness could not be so. Lord of Laṅkā, that suchness should be understood to be a figurative designation that does not fall under the power of anything. Therefore, Lord of Laṅkā, all phenomena are nameless, wordless, inexpressible; the path of words is cut off."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, is there not even the conception of a mere meaning as a basis for what is designated by a name?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, there is no conception even of a mere meaning as a basis. There is no conceptualization at all."

"Why is that? Because even the Buddha himself, though he searches, does not find any mere meaning as a basis of which one could say, 'This is it.' Therefore, conceptualizing adventitiously and not conceptualizing are merely designated as sentient being and buddha; in suchness itself there is not the slightest buddha or sentient being. Yet childish beings, obscured by the clouds of the adventitious, make adventitious phenomena their concern and conceive of saṃsāra and awakening as two. They conceive of sentient beings and buddhas as two."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, then how should these two, sentient beings and buddhas, be understood?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, they should be understood as emptiness."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, in what does emptiness abide?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, emptiness abides in all phenomena."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, in what do all phenomena abide?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, because all phenomena are nothing but emptiness, they abide in nothing at all."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, abiding in emptiness, in what does one abide?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, to abide in emptiness is to abide in great loving-kindness; it is to abide in that emptiness by which the welfare of sentient beings is brought to complete fulfillment. Why is that? Because for buddhas and sentient beings there is no meaning whatever apart from emptiness."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, if buddhas and sentient beings are all empty, whose great compassion acts upon whom, and how? Of what is that great compassion a figurative expression?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, because all phenomena abide by their very nature in emptiness, they are not seen as two, and so compassion has no object. This is great compassion, because it delivers all beings completely to the heart of awakening. But if, with the compassion of childish beings, one were to rescue the lives of the beings of a great trichiliocosm and bring them out to freedom, that should be understood to be lesser compassion. Lord of Laṅkā, therefore one should abide by abiding continually in great compassion.

"Lord of Laṅkā, those who abide in great compassion neither liberate any being nor bind any; theirs is a killing with the vajra weapon in which there is no actual killing at all. Why is that? Because, from the absence of sentient beings, sentient beings are designated. That great compassion should be understood to be a figurative expression for the distinction between suchness and the adventitious.

"Lord of Laṅkā, therefore it is like this:

All phenomena designated by limitless names,
Seen as existent, appearing and renowned,
Are merely designated as figurative labels:
How could there be any actual basis in them?

The basis on which names are designated
Is a nonexistent cause; imputed, clung to and pursued, it is accomplished,
And appears wherever one looks.
Therefore, with nothing to be done, all is already done.

From what does not appear, all things appear;
Each and every thing appears adventitiously.
Appearance itself is the heart of nonexistence,
And is not apprehended as either existent or nonexistent.

From the absence of buddhas, the various figurative names
Such as buddha, awakening,
The nature of reality, and so forth,
Are designated in order to teach the ignorant and deluded.

In that very thing, the figurative names
Of convention such as buddha,
Awakening, the nature of reality, and so forth,
Are without name and are not expressed in words.

From the vast center of the sky of the nature of reality,
The sun body of wisdom, shining clearly,
With the light rays of the lamp of compassion,
Dispels the darkness of delusion and illuminates all."

When he had said this, the Lord of Laṅkā and the other embodied beings found relief, without hope or striving toward anything else.

This concludes the eighth chapter, Showing the Very Essence of Secret Mantra, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

From the Way of Going Forth in the Great Vehicle, the account of the Complete Array of the Lamp, a discussion of the wisdom of the Dharma seal: this is the second part, showing how it came about.

Here the second fascicle begins.

Then the Lord of Laṅkā again questioned the Lord of Secrets about what had been taught earlier as the third of the twelve statements about past events, that "it has also arisen when someone gains certainty." He asked, "O Lord of Secrets, when you yourself showed the way the supreme secret of the Great Vehicle came about and told it in your prophecy, you set out that 'it has also arisen when someone gains certainty.' In what way is that gained, so that it is spoken of?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, this refers to that very suchness in which someone gains unmistaken certainty about the suchness of Vajrasattva, the essence of secret mantra."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, from where did Vajrasattva, the essence of secret mantra, arise? Where does he abide? Where does he appear? How did he arise?"

When he had asked this, the Lord of Secrets said, "O Lord of Laṅkā, Vajrasattva, the very essence of secret mantra, arose from the element of both saṃsāra and nirvāṇa. He abides in the heart of all buddhas and the mind of all sentient beings. He appears in limitless fields. He is self-arisen as primordially just as he is and as purified in the mindstream."

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, on what should one settle with certainty as Vajrasattva himself?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, Vajrasattva is free from any essential nature of being one or different. The nature of reality of phenomena, uncontrived and unadulterated, abiding primordially at the heart of awakening, is designated Vajrasattva primordially just as he is. The occasion on which one sees the nature of reality of the minds of all sentient beings is designated Vajrasattva of the purified mindstream. Likewise, all buddhas too appear as buddhas primordially just as they are and as buddhas of the purified mindstream. Therefore certainty about Vajrasattva should be settled on the suchness of all things, primordially just as it is."

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, what is the nature of reality of the minds of all sentient beings? Seeing what, does one see it?"

When he had asked this, the Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, if one sees the way all sentient beings are, one sees it. The way all sentient beings are is unsurpassed awakening. In it not even an atom of any phenomenon is apprehended. It has the characteristic of space. In it there is no one who awakens and nothing to be awakened to. In it there are no sentient beings and no designation as sentient beings. There is no bearer of qualities and no designation as a bearer of qualities."

"Why is that? Because all phenomena have the characteristic of awakening, and awakening has the characteristic of space, and space is free of all characteristics. Therefore awakening is to be sought in the mind. Because that mind is by nature completely pure, it is not within, it is not outside, and it is not apprehended in between the two. That mind has not been seen, is not seen, and will not be seen even by the buddhas.

"How is it that they have not seen it? They have not seen it as a color. They have not seen it as a shape. They have not seen it as darkness. They have not seen it as light. They have not seen it as male or female. They have not seen it as neuter. They have not seen it as having the nature of the three realms. They have not seen it as the mind of a god. They have not seen it as the mind of a nāga. Likewise they have not seen it as the mind of any of the different classes of beings: the mind of a yakṣa, the mind of a gandharva, of an asura, a garuḍa, a kinnara, a mahoraga, a human or a nonhuman, an animal, a hungry ghost, a hell being and so forth. It is free of all thought and conceptuality. Why is that? Because the nature of mind is the self-arisen wisdom of Vajrasattva, and that wisdom has the nature of space, since it has no intrinsic nature."

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "O Lord of Secrets, why is the mind of sentient beings the self-arisen wisdom of Vajrasattva?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, the suchness of sentient beings and the suchness of Vajrasattva are not two and cannot be made two. How is that? It is like this. The suchness of a mind of attachment is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The suchness of a mind without attachment is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The reality of a mind of aversion is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The suchness of a mind of loving-kindness is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The suchness of a mind of delusion is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The suchness of great wisdom is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The suchness of a mind of doubt is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The suchness of a mind of certainty is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The suchness of a mind of darkness is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The suchness of a mind of light is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The suchness of a mind of pride is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The suchness of a mind of jealousy is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality. The suchness of a mind of gathering in is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality.

"Likewise, the reality of a mind of sending out, the reality of a mind of dividing, the reality of an agitated mind, the reality of a peaceful mind, the reality of a mind of fighting, the reality of a mind of joining together, the reality of a mind of disputing, the reality of a mind in harmony, the reality of a speaking mind, the reality of a mind without speech, the reality of the mind of a god, the reality of the mind of a nāga, the reality of the mind of a māra, the reality of the mind of an asura, the reality of the mind of a yakṣa, the reality of the mind of a human, the reality of the mind of a mahoraga, the reality of the mind of a bhūta, the reality of the mind of a garuḍa, the reality of the mind of a gandharva, the reality of the mind of a kumbhāṇḍa, the reality of the mind of a man, the reality of the mind of a woman, the reality of the mind of a neuter, the reality of one's own mind, the reality of another's mind, the reality of a father's mind, the reality of a mother's mind, the reality of a child's mind, the reality of a sibling's mind, the reality of an enemy's mind, the reality of the mind of Īśvara, the reality of the mind of Brahmā, the reality of the mind of Desiring Inclusion, the reality of the mind of Indra, the reality of the mind of Gaṇapati, the reality of the mind of a wandering ascetic, the reality of the mind of a merchant, the reality of the mind of a farmer, the reality of a mind like a river, the reality of a mind like a pond, the reality of a mind like a well, the reality of a mind like an array, the reality of a mind like a wheel, the reality of a mind like a water wheel, the reality of a mind like boiling water, the reality of a mind like molten gold, the reality of a mind that guards everything, the reality of a mind like stinginess, the reality of a mind like giving, the reality of a mind like a cat, the reality of a mind like a descending flame, the reality of a mind like lightning, the reality of a mind like a cloud, the reality of a mind like a mouse, the reality of a mind like a dog, the reality of a mind like a song,

the reality of a mind like a dance, the reality of a mind like cymbals, the reality of a mind like a peacock, the reality of a mind like the sound of a drum, and a mind like seasonal thunder, a mind like a lion, a mind like a bumblebee, a mind like a bee, a mind like a sea monster, a mind like a turtle, a mind like an emanation, a mind like a buffalo, a mind like an owl, a mind like a monkey, a mind like a crow, a mind like a horse, a mind like a goose, a mind like a heron, the mind of a wolf, the mind of a fox, a mind like an elephant, a mind like a sheep, a mind like a hawk, the mind of a householder, the mind of the homeless, the mind of the liberated, the mind of the unliberated, the mind of a rākṣasa, the mind of a wild man, the mind of a young girl, the mind of a king, the mind of a prostitute, the mind of a harlot, the mind of a brahmin, the mind of a butcher, the mind of a hungry ghost, the mind of a hell being, the mind of a thorn, the mind of cotton, the mind of the underworld, the mind of the sky, the mind of a net, the mind of a snare, the mind of a noose, the mind of a ligri, the mind of a hook, the mind of a hammer, the mind of a ladle, the mind of a vessel, the mind of a khrimön, the mind of a flame, the mind of an ember, the mind of a lamp, the mind of the sun, the mind of the moon, the mind of silver, the mind of gold, the mind of a spark, the mind of wind, the mind of water, the mind of a spear shaft, the mind of a pestle, the mind of a door, the mind of a plank, the mind of fire, the mind of earth, the mind of mud, the mind of muddy water, the mind of pigment, the mind of a jewel, the mind of poison, the mind of medicine, the mind of food, the mind of a pit, the mind of an arrow, the mind of a sword, the mind of a wheel, the mind of a bow, the mind of a fetter, the mind of wood, the mind of salt, the mind of a razor, the mind of a measuring line, the mind of a vajra, the mind of Mount Meru, the mind of the ocean, the mind of a boulder, the mind of a maternal uncle, the mind of a cave, the mind of birth, the mind of a flower, the mind of fruit, the mind of a root, the mind of a branch, the mind of a stalk, the mind of sweetness, the mind of bitterness, the mind of sourness, the mind of pungency, the mind of mildness, the mind of astringency, the mind of letters, the mind of a clay votive image, the mind of a key, a mind like a jagak bird, a mind like a park, a mind like a summer cloud, a mind like a mirror, a mind like sesame oil, a mind like winter beer, and so on: the suchness of all these limitless minds is the suchness of Vajrasattva. The suchness of Vajrasattva is reality just as it is.

"O Lord of Laṅkā, why is that? Because the suchness of Vajrasattva is uncontrived and unadulterated, unmade and unexamined, just as it is; and the suchness of the minds of all sentient beings too is uncontrived, unadulterated, unmade and unexamined, reality just as it is. Even the eye of omniscience has not seen, does not see, and will not see the natures of these two as separate. Even the discerning wisdom of the regent beings has not distinguished, does not distinguish, and will not distinguish them as separate.

"O Lord of Laṅkā, those who wish to gain certainty about secret mantra should recognize the reality of mind. Why is that? Because the minds that appear as different, as they do for a yogin of ritual activity, appear as the single reality of mind, as they do for a yogin beyond activity; and since that single reality is the supreme secret reality, even Vajrasattva, the heart of all buddhas, gained certainty about secret mantra by realizing the reality of mind. How is that? The reality of mind is the gateway of complete purity. It is the gateway of the light of the Dharma. It is the expanse of phenomena. It is the great volume of the Dharma. It is the great samādhi of the stream of the Dharma.

"O Lord of Laṅkā, it is also like this:

Whoever finds the reality of mind,
That secret essence,
Even if it is not explained in the words of the Guide,
Has dharmas arising in the mind by countless hundreds of thousands;
At the doorway of speech all becomes clear.
The reasons that bring certainty about the meaning
They are able to express and bring forth in words.
They have attained the samādhi of the stream of the Dharma.

"O Lord of Laṅkā, thus the reality of mind is great awakening, and great awakening is that of Vajrasattva. It is also the nectar of all buddhas, like this:

Without the water of the awakening mind,
Even the victors would dry up.
This river of the awakening mind
Moistens the parched banks of former clinging to extremes.

Because the nature of mind is emptiness,
All that arises from it is empty.
Because that is empty, awakening too
Is nothing but the essence of emptiness.

The great water of self-arisen wisdom:
From the center of the expanse of the wondrous ocean
Flows the water of the nectar of awakening,
Which cools all the victors.

This is the secret essence,
The very nature ascertained
As the reality of Vajrasattva,
Praised as supreme by all the victors.

"Therefore, Lord of Laṅkā, to recognize the reality of mind is the great affair of the realm."

When the Lord of Secrets had spoken thus, everyone entered the path of complete liberation into the reality of Vajrasattva.

This concludes the ninth chapter, Showing the Reality of Vajrasattva, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā asked the Lord of Secrets, "Lord of Secrets, whose domain of experience is that essence of secret mantra, the reality of mind?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, it is the domain of experience of the buddhas, the blessed ones, but not of those who dwell on the grounds, one after another. Why is that? Because it is thus difficult to examine, difficult to teach, and free of all conventions of expression."
