Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "O Lord of Secrets, if that reality of mind, the essence of secret mantra, is not shared with others, apart from being the domain of experience of the sublime ones, then consider the yogins who engage in practice. When they too look at the reality of mind and search throughout the mind stream, at that time they do not see an inner mind, they do not see an outer mind, and they do not see a mind both inner and outer. They do not see mind in the aggregates, they do not see it in the elements, and they do not see it in the sense sources. Not seeing mind in this way, when they look into where this mind comes from, they reflect thus: mind arises because there is an object of focus. And they reflect further:

Is that object of focus one thing,
and that mind something else?
Is the object of focus itself the mind?
Is the mind itself the object of focus?
If the object of focus is one thing,
and the mind is something else,

then reality is not one but becomes two. But if they are one, then it is not reasonable that mind should follow and look at mind, or that mind should see mind. Just as, for example, a sword cannot cut itself, and the tip of a finger cannot touch itself, so that mind cannot take that mind as its object. When they look in this way, they see it thus: it is independent; mind has no location and no support; it is not annihilated and not permanent; it is not heaped together; it does not arise from causes and is not produced by conditions; it is not from itself and not from another; it is not the same and not other; it has no class and no cause; it does not radiate and does not appear; it is without essential nature. They see it just as it is. It is genuine, unmoving and secluded. They see not even the slightest thing, and do not see anything distinctly. They do not conceive and do not discriminate; they do not stir, do not think, and do not emanate. Mind is formless, and whatever arises is also formless. Since in that there is no form, awakening too has no form. The nature of mind is awakening. The nature of awakening is all phenomena. The nature of all phenomena is reality.

"This mind is thus swift, extremely swift, unabiding, roving, like a monkey, like the wind, like a mirage, like a stream of water, like a lamp, like the image of a body; it travels far, it moves nowhere at all, it turns back, it dwells on objects, it ranges over the six, it knows everything, it is capable of one-pointedness, it does not arise, it arises as everything, it abides in calm, it is diverse yet one, undistracted and wholly distracted. They know mind to be equal in the three times, and know mind to be reality. Just as they know their own mind, so they fully know the minds of others. Just as their own mind is empty, so they fully know the minds of all to be empty. Just as they know their own mind to be equal, so they fully know the minds of all to be equal. Thereby they fully know all phenomena to be equal. Because all phenomena are equal, they fully know awakening to be equal. Thereby they fully know mind as the expanse of all phenomena. 'That expanse of all is not something common to all,' they know. Since they thus abide in mind, practice in mind, have mastery over mind, look at mind, see mind, and recognize mind, Lord of Secrets, why do you say that it is the domain of experience of the sublime ones alone, and not the domain of experience of those on the grounds?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, that is indeed so; but all of them, in every respect, have ascertained the reality of mind only in terms of the characteristic of the cause, and do not fully know it as the result itself. Why is that? It is like this. Those on the grounds, one after another, are entirely caught within the mesh of four nets. What are the four? The great net, the net, the half net, and the single net. These are none other than the childish, the mad, those of small perception, and those of single perception. Those on the grounds, because they know nothing beyond mind as cause alone, are those of single perception. They are caught in the net, purely bound by the net, and so it is extremely difficult for them to find certainty in the result. Why is that? Lord of Laṅkā, that which is the reality of mind as result cannot be spoken even by the buddhas, and cannot be indicated even by Vajrasattva; what need is there to mention bodhisattvas?"

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked, "Lord of Secrets, have even you yourself not found certainty in the reality of mind as result?" When he had asked this, the Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, since I myself have not yet found certainty, it is extremely difficult to teach it to others. Why is that? Because it has become the domain of the intention of the Blessed One Vajrasattva alone."

When the Lord of Secrets had spoken thus, the awestruck presumption of all who were mistaken about awakening was utterly cut off.

This concludes the tenth chapter, The Supreme Distinction, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then the Lord of Laṅkā and the other beings embodied among the destinies were amazed and astonished, and they said this: "O Lord of Secrets, as all the buddhas, the blessed ones, have declared, the Lord of Secrets is the one who cuts off all doubts concerning the unerring reality, the sublime secret of Vajrasattva, the lord of all tathāgatas. If, then, you neither teach nor practice the secret reality of the result, which is so extremely wondrous, when will we ever find certainty?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "O beings of inferior bodies, even Vajrasattva himself, sovereign of all tathāgatas, until he found the reality of secret mantra, practiced immeasurable austerities for some three countless eons, and yet did not know it in all its aspects."

"How is that so? Just as the countless buddhas of fully purified mind stream who have appeared and will appear practice austerities until they find certainty in reality, so it is. Thus, after this Fortunate Eon, sixty degenerate eons of barbarism will occur. After that, in the eon called Renowned, ten thousand buddhas will appear. After that, eighty thousand degenerate eons will occur. After that, in the eon Like Scattered Stars, eighty thousand buddhas will appear. After that, three hundred eons of darkness will occur. After that, in the eon called Beautiful Ornament, eighty-five thousand buddhas will appear. Likewise, without end, eons of lamps and eons of darkness follow one another forever, in a continuous cycle. Thus in this very Fortunate Eon one thousand and two buddhas appear.

"Before this Fortunate Eon there were a hundred eons of darkness. Before that, in the eon called Endowed with Perfection, a hundred koṭis of buddhas appeared. Before that had passed, there were a hundred eons of barbarism. Before that had passed, in the eon called Excellent Ornament, eighty-four koṭis of buddhas appeared. Before that had passed, there were five hundred eons of darkness. Before that had passed, in the eon called Delightful to See, ten koṭis of buddhas appeared. Before that had passed, there were seven hundred eons of darkness. Before that had passed, in the eon called Joy, sixty thousand buddhas appeared. In such a way, endlessly, eons of darkness and eons of lamps have followed one another and still follow one another, without beginning or end. And however many lamps have appeared and will appear, until they find the definitive reality of secret mantra, though they practice immeasurable austerities, they did not know, do not know, and will not know all its aspects. As soon as they find the definitive reality of secret mantra, they knew all its aspects, they know them, and they will know them.

"Likewise, many immeasurable and inconceivable eons ago, countless eons beyond even the reckoning of former eons, in the great eon called Manifest Joy, at the time when thirty-three thousand buddhas appeared, in the teaching of the last buddha, called Samantabhadra, five great supreme ones arose from the five castes, the royal caste, the commoner caste, the merchant caste, the brahmin caste, and the caṇḍāla caste. They were the boy called Bliss Protector, the boy called Tralita, the boy called Śūradatta, the boy Karmadatta, and the boy called Dumbur. At that time, on that occasion, the buddha Vajrasattva was the boy called Bliss Protector. At that time, on that occasion, the buddha Amitāyus was the boy called Tralita. At that time, on that occasion, the buddha Ratnasambhava was the boy called Śūradatta. At that time, on that occasion, the buddha Vairocana was the boy called Karmadatta. At that time, on that occasion, the buddha Amoghasiddhi was the boy called Dumbur.

"At that time, on that occasion, when those boys generated the mind of supreme yoga before that buddha Samantabhadra, at the worldly stage they generated eight minds, and, having traversed by way of the eight worldly paths, they dwelt on the ground of uncertain transformation. At that time, though they cut their doubts before thirty-three thousand buddhas, they did not find certainty. At the stage of the beginner they generated three minds, mastered the ten extremes, and, relying on this, dwelt on the ground of the basis. At that time, on that occasion, though they cut their doubts before forty-four thousand buddhas, they did not find certainty. At the stage of opening the great lineage, four minds were perfected; having mastered the ten extremes, they became bestowers of merit. At that time, though they cut their doubts before fifty-five thousand buddhas, they did not find certainty. Again, at the stage of attaining great confidence, one mind was perfected; having mastered the ten extremes and passed beyond the five fears, relying on this, they dwelt on the ground of special progress. At that time, by cutting their doubts before sixty-six thousand buddhas, they knew a mere portion and attained confidence. This is as follows:

That person who trains in illusion,
in the guise of a universal monarch,
with the seven precious things and with armies,
teaches the vehicle of the means of ruling with power.

"Again, at the stage of obtaining the great prophecy, seven minds were perfected; with the coexisting stains purified, they crossed beyond the mountain, and through focus dwelt on the ground where the result arises. At that time, by hearing the prophecy from seventy-seven thousand buddhas, they dwelt in great realization. This is as follows:

At that time that sublime being,
knowing the path within the result itself,
the limbs of the path perfected in bliss,
in order to bring others to maturity,
applies the supreme means just as is wished.

"Again, at the stage of perfecting great power, they generated three minds and, having mastered the six extremes, dwelt on the ground of not remaining fixed. At that time, having heard prophecies from eighty-eight thousand buddhas, they gained assurance. This is as follows:

Then that person, the regent
of whichever Dharma king it may be,
sustaining his line, teaches the Great Vehicle in Tuṣita,
and, unabiding, displays miracles in the ten directions and the times.

"Lord of Laṅkā, thus those beginner sons of noble family took birth in the five castes. Then, again, beyond inexpressible immeasurable eons, at a time without beginning or end, they generated the supreme mind of awakening before the five buddhas, and were born as the five sons of the universal monarch Vairocana, namely the five called Beautiful Flower, Inconceivable Glory, Holder of the Wealth Giver, Glory of the Crescent Moon, and Light Beautiful to All. Among them, as soon as they became yogins of the beginner's aspiration, they obtained what is called mental engagement. Through that they saw the sign of traveling through the sky. Then, as soon as they became yogins of the opening of the great lineage, they obtained the samādhi of radiating and gathering as one. Through that they saw the sign of themselves appearing as a garland of letters. Then, as soon as they appeared as yogins of great confidence, they obtained the samādhi of irreversibility."

"Through that they saw the sign of moving about in a world system filled with precious jewels. Then, as soon as they appeared as yogins of the great prophecy, they obtained the samādhi of suchness. Through that they saw the sign of riding upon lions and overpowering everything. Then, as soon as they appeared as yogins of perfected great power, they obtained the samādhi of total illumination. Through that they saw the sign of turning the wheels of the sun and moon and making everything bright with light.

"Lord of Laṅkā, even though they had become thus, they had not found certainty in the reality that is the essence of secret mantra. Why is that? Lord of Laṅkā, when those beginner sons of noble family became regent persons, they were renowned throughout all buddha fields as the holy being Vajrasattva, the holy being Vajraratna, the holy being Vajradharma, the holy being Karmavajra, and the holy being Vajradhātu. In some places they were spoken of and renowned as regent persons. In some places they were spoken of and renowned as those in their final existence. In some places they were spoken of and renowned as buddhas of the great ground. Yet that was because they had not yet experienced the sublime secret intention of the buddhas, and still needed to settle in equipoise.

"Why is that? Since those holy beings had not experienced the intention of the buddhas, for as long as they had not settled in equipoise, through genuine realization they made this request to the primordial buddha Vajrasattva: 'Blessed One, the buddhas, the blessed ones, have neither being settled in equipoise nor not being settled, and know all aspects. Yet although we have completed all the immeasurable austerities that are to be performed over immeasurable eons, we have not come to experience the intention of the buddhas, for as long as we have not settled in equipoise. This is a wonder. Sugata, this is a wonder.'

"When they had made this request, the Blessed One, that being of vajra essence, declared: 'O holy beings, those who wish to be spontaneously accomplished as the very state of a tathāgata must bring the qualities of a buddha to full completion in every respect by means of three things to be perfected. Of these, you have fully completed what is to be done, and you have fully purified your guarding; yet you have not yet brought your understanding to full comprehension. Therefore, bring your understanding to full comprehension of the primordial reality of the holy ones. As that becomes fully comprehended, you will experience the intention of the holy ones.'

"When he had declared this, the holy beings made a request: 'How wonderful, Blessed One! Since the Blessed One has spoken to us of something so extremely wondrous and marvelous, hard to fathom, giving rise to doubt and bringing about hesitation, please explain what this is that is called the reality of the holy ones, which brings understanding to full comprehension. Sugata, please explain it.'

"When they had made this request, that Blessed One of all the buddhas, the reality that is the essence of secret mantra, declared: 'O holy beings, this reality is great in means and wide in its gateways, small in hardship and great in purpose, near at hand yet hard to examine, great in danger and hard to guard. Therefore it is very difficult for me to teach it to you without distortion.' When he had declared this, those holy beings wept until they were utterly spent. Having wept with great intensity, the door of the secret was opened, and they came to know fully the eight topics of secret mantra."

This concludes the eleventh chapter, Showing the Lineage of Births of the Purified Mind Stream, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then the Blessed One of all the tathāgatas, Vajrasattva, the reality of the holy ones, spoke thus to those holy beings: "O holy beings, do not weep! Do not give rise to the torment of suffering! Why do you weep?"

Then those holy beings spoke thus to the Blessed One of all the tathāgatas, Vajrasattva, the reality: "Blessed One, how could we not weep? Has our search not come to nothing? Blessed One, what are we to do with our understanding? Please be our holy spiritual friend. Although we have practiced immeasurable austerities for countless ages, single-mindedly, wishing for full comprehension, if we still have not even the fortune to hear the mere name of the reality that is the essence of secret mantra, how can this be?"

When they had made this request, the Blessed One of all the tathāgatas, Vajrasattva, the reality, declared: "Holy beings, do not speak like that. Secret mantra is not common to all. Its meaning, reality, is the sublime intention of the buddhas alone: inexpressible, free of all thought and analysis, impossible to point out as 'this', difficult to examine, difficult to teach, free of all conventions of expression, beyond example, not comparable even to space, measureless, beyond measure, not to be gauged by valid cognition, by reasoning, or by inference. Just as you have neither known nor heard it, so even all the buddhas and bodhisattvas of fully purified mind stream have not yet directly seen, directly realized, or become manifestly and perfectly awakened to the reality that is the essence of secret mantra. Why is that? Because the reality that is the essence of secret mantra is completely pure, it is not perceived as 'buddhas' or 'bodhisattvas', and it cannot be sought. The holy beings who dwell on the tenth ground do not know that the sublime reality that is the essence of secret mantra, the buddha-nature, is present in themselves; they set it up as the nature of reality and are not free of analysis. The buddhas dwell unmoving in the state free of conceptual thought at the heart of awakening, dwelling without manifesting full awakening. So why do you weep?"

When he had declared this, those holy beings were delighted, overjoyed and elated, and, taking heart, they thought thus:

"If even the buddhas, the blessed ones, who have gone before have not yet actualized the reality that is the essence of secret mantra, what need is there to speak of the likes of us? But here is the Teacher, Vajrasattva, the reality, who is none other than the holy spiritual friend of all the buddhas; so there will surely be no delay in our understanding coming to comprehend this."

Then those holy beings made this request to that Blessed One, the reality: "Blessed One, it may be said that the sublime reality of secret mantra is beyond all expression and cannot be spoken, and so is extremely difficult to teach and to examine. Even so, since the Blessed One himself is certain as the domain of those of great understanding, cuts off all doubts, and is the most holy of the holy, please teach us the sublime side by which understanding definitively comprehends, in the sublime equality of the intention."

When they had made this request, that Blessed One, the reality, declared: "Holy beings, excellent! That is certain. It is like this. That sublime reality of secret mantra is free of all analysis. In it no extreme or middle is perceived. It is without superimposition or denial, without accepting or rejecting. It is beyond cause and result. It is primordially pure, primordially complete. There is nothing to do, nothing to seek, nothing to accomplish, nothing to undertake. It does not come from elsewhere. It is without antidote, without agent, unmade, unexamined, selfless, uncontrived, unadulterated, certain to be just that, unmistaken, as it is, unceasing, unborn, not setting, not perishing, not appearing, not arisen, not going, abiding primordially, pervading all, without name, without form, without sign, without convention, not to be looked at, not to be grasped, not to be indicated, without example, without measure, not to be examined, beyond speculation, immeasurable, unthinkable, inconceivable, the source of all, in every respect great equanimity, emptiness, nothing but one's own self-arisen awareness."

When he had declared this, the holy beings understood that in all phenomena there is no accepting or rejecting. Wonder arose in them, and then doubt. "How wonderful! As we heard before from the holy ones, the holy ones of old take up awakening and cast off the afflictions, and that is called the full comprehension of understanding. Having heard that, it is a wonder that this great being says that, since afflictions and awakening are not two, there is nothing to accept or reject." Thinking this, they remained without saying anything at all.

Then Vajrasattva, the reality, in order to clear away the thoughts of those holy beings, spoke again: "O holy beings, since you are wholly consumed by doubt, discern with certainty what the nature of the afflictions is and where they come from, and what the nature of awakening is and where it comes from. As soon as you see this fully, your doubt will be cut off."

When he had said this, those holy beings examined what the nature of afflictions and of awakening was and where they came from. They saw that their nature is empty. They saw that they arise from the root, the mind. Mind itself has not arisen; it is unborn and without birth, primordially as it is, its nature spontaneously accomplished. They came to understand with certainty that the root is nothing other than the sublime awakening mind, and they experienced the intention of the buddhas. It was like this: all mind and mental factors became like space. They passed beyond being settled in equipoise and not being settled, attained final confidence, and fully found genuine certainty; they came to find it. Having found it completely, those holy beings again wept until they were utterly spent.

Again Vajrasattva, the reality, though he knew, said to those holy beings: "O holy beings, why are you weeping?"

They replied, "Holy spiritual friend, it is like this:

The shortcut among the paths,
this sublime one among the means,
we had not heard of before: small was our fortune.
How wonderful, this marvelous Dharma!
Though a meaning of such lasting worth as this
depends on nothing else at all,
we hoped that others would bestow it. Alas, what error!
Worn out by the labor of things to be done,
distracted, chasing after signs,
we were wearied by pointless, endless words,
not gathered into summary, each of little meaning.
From you, holy spiritual friend,
having found certainty, we breathe a great sigh of relief:
equal to the buddhas, liberated into the expanse,
spontaneously accomplished as great suchness."

So they said. From their great wondrous joy, a light of wisdom arose like a hundred thousand suns. Through it those on the grounds trained the dynamism of awareness, and the greatness of the meaning came forth. All the lands shook, so that the yogins of conduct were amazed and asked their own teachers, "What is this extraordinary sign?" When they asked this, the countless individual teachers declared to their countless individual retinues: "Sons of noble family, this great sign is because, in the buddha field called Great Pervasion, the spiritual friend of all the buddhas, called Vajrasattva, the reality, is teaching the Dharma called The Certainty of the Reality of Secret Mantra of the Great Vehicle. That buddha cuts off all doubts and has become the holy spiritual friend who prophesies that all buddhas and bodhisattvas will certainly awaken. Five holy beings, who had not found the certainty of secret mantra, have become fully, manifestly and perfectly awakened, because he cut their doubts concerning certainty and gave them prophecy. That is their sign." So they taught.

"Lord of Laṅkā, thus those holy beings, as soon as they found the experience of reality, were definitively known as the holy regent persons of the Dharma king."

This concludes the twelfth chapter, called Fully Attaining Great Confidence, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then the Lord of Laṅkā, the bodhisattva great being, asked this question: "Lord of Secrets, how are those holy beings renowned in the fields of the holy ones?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, because those holy beings became the regents of the Dharma king in the fields of the holy ones, they became universally renowned as the regent persons."

"Why is that? Lord of Laṅkā, it is like this. For example, great personages who have become the crown of all, when they are born in the line of royal kings, possess the marks, know the law, have a retinue, and have gained power, receive the reverence of many. Just so, those holy beings too were born in the line of all the victors, possess the signs of all the major and minor marks, know the Dharma, have a retinue gathered to them, and have gained the ten masteries. Because they possess these qualities, they are renowned as the regent persons. Lord of Laṅkā, this is also as follows:

Just as one born in a royal line,
when the people entrust the kingdom to him,
rules the land with firm command,
having become the crown of all,
so when the victors are fully complete,
the world together with its gods
invests them with sovereignty as Dharma kings,
and then they manifestly awaken to awakening.

"Lord of Laṅkā, thus, at the very place where those holy beings endowed with the five qualities fully attained great confidence, countless victors from the endless fields of the holy ones gathered together, like masses of summer clouds in the sky, and all of them with one voice conferred on them the empowerment of renown, of the name Holy Regent Person, Dharma King.

"Thus some conferred empowerment by the empowerment into the vajra lineage and its line of succession. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment into the jewel lineage and its line of succession. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment into the lotus lineage and its line of succession. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment into the youth lineage and its line of succession. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment into the elephant lineage and its line of succession. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the light rays of the major marks. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the light rays of the minor marks. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the light rays of the ornaments. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the light rays of six colors. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the unshared qualities. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the ten powers. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the four fearlessnesses. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the individual perfect knowledges. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of concordance. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of gathering through impartiality. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of gathering through giving. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of gathering through conduct. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of concordant conduct. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of gathering through guidance. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of gathering through mastery. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of gathering through equality. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of gathering through dependence. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of gathering through the cause. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of gathering through the result. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of mastery wherever one may be. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of cognizance. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of joy coming under one's control. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of complete array. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of entering everything. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of total swiftness. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of abiding just as things abide. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of acting just as things are. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of seeing without arising or ceasing. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of appearing as anything at all. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the regent person. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of blessing. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of prophecy. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the capacity of great power. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the river of the continuum. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the river of arising. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the river of renown. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the river of perfection.

"Lord of Laṅkā, as for those holy beings, some empowered them as Dharma kings. Some empowered them as lords of blessing. Some empowered them for the task of acting as lamps. Some empowered them as holders of the secret. Some empowered them after showing them to be like the Dharma king himself. Some empowered them after showing them to be like the buddha of the family. Some empowered them after showing them as many as the countless victors. Some empowered them after showing them to be like the two form bodies. Some empowered them because the time had come to ascend the royal seat. Some empowered them because the time had come to awaken in form. Some empowered them because the time had come to attain prophecy with certainty. Some empowered them because the time had come for certainty in the secret essence. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment like a jeweled crest. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment of the blazing vajra of sun and moon. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment like the seal of the family. Some conferred empowerment by the empowerment like a vajra diamond. Some conferred empowerment in settings purely like Tuṣita. Some conferred empowerment in settings purely like Akaniṣṭha. Some conferred empowerment at the very place of the heart of awakening. Some conferred empowerment as in the palaces of the heaven of mastery over others' emanations and the like. Some bestowed what is like the regency of the Dharma. Some bestowed what is like perfect enjoyment. Some bestowed what is like being the very sovereign of the Dharma. Some bestowed what is like being the Lord of Secrets.

"Lord of Laṅkā, the meaning of empowerment:
cause, attainment, and non-attainment,
object, vessel, distinction, and sign;
by these seven its meaning is summarized.

"Lord of Laṅkā, of these, the cause is the five great yogas. They should be known as the yoga of aspiration, the yoga of opening the great lineage, the yoga of great confidence, the yoga of obtaining the great prophecy, and the yoga of perfecting great power.

"Lord of Laṅkā, the measure of attainment is this: full comprehension of the trainings, seeing reality, not being daunted by any object of knowledge, and freedom from all fear. What does not attain it is conceit, uncertainty, fixation on something as sublime, and that in which it does not arise.

"Lord of Laṅkā, the objects are the stūpa, the echo, valid cognition, and certainty.

"Lord of Laṅkā, what has become a vessel is one who is born in the lineage, who has perfected great power, in whom compassion has arisen, and who has brought others under his influence."

"Lord of Laṅkā, the distinction is this: what is obtained through power, and what is obtained through conferral.

"Lord of Laṅkā, what is obtained through power is this:

Whoever, having realized the secret mind of the victors,
Makes it clear to others,
Holds the supreme of all empowerments:
The three realms of existence empower that one.

"Lord of Laṅkā, what is obtained through conferral should be understood as being only as far as it benefits someone and as far as one is able.

"Lord of Laṅkā, the signs are these:

The arising of the sublime crown protuberance,
A vividness that rests on nothing,
Spontaneous and complete accomplishment,
And the spreading of infinite rays of light.

"Lord of Laṅkā, since this is so, those holy beings are renowned throughout the fields of the holy ones as 'sublime kings of Dharma.'

"Lord of Laṅkā, as for empowerment too,
Gather your mind to one point and understand it:
Its source and the river of its renown,
Its object, its faculties, and its purpose,
Its means, its basis, its signs, and its meaning:
With this summary it is complete."

This concludes the thirteenth chapter, on holy beings obtaining empowerment, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then Vajrasattva, suchness itself, prophesied the full perfection of those holy beings, as follows: "You holy beings have, from a time without beginning or end, been nothing other than fully and perfectly awakened buddhas. The holy being called Sattvavajra will become the buddha Akṣobhya in the world realm called Manifest Joy. The holy being called Vajraratna will appear as the buddha called Ratnasambhava in the world realm called Glorious. The holy being called Vajradharma will appear as the buddha called Amitābha in the world realm called Sukhāvatī. The holy being called Vajradhātu will appear as the buddha called Vairocana in the world realm called Adorned with Awakening. The holy being called Viśvavajra will appear as the buddha called Amoghasiddhi in the world realm called Utterly Pure.

"The qualities of all those buddha fields lie beyond any example by which they might be described, and since there is no example, they cannot be told. The measure of those buddhas' lives is simply immeasurable. The great beings who dwell in those buddha fields are great vidyādharas alone, and the measure of their lives is the life span of the vidyādharas of that very field. Countless vidyādharas who have ripened in other world realms also appear in those buddha fields. There, listening to the Dharma, teaching it, and moving about are samādhi alone. The Dharma, too, is nothing but the supreme secret: the actionless nature of reality, suchness. Their enjoyment, too, is the enjoyment of the profound nature of reality alone.

"All those vidyādharas belong only to the five families. Why is this? All arise from the five families, appear as five, and ripen as five; having come from five, they are definitely five. That is to say, they belong only to the family of anger, the family of pride, the family of desire, the family of jealousy, and the family of delusion; the Forbearing family, the All-Tormented family, the Six-Arising family, the Lineage-Holding family, and the Abode-Holding family; the commoner caste, the royal caste, the merchant caste, the brahmin caste, and the caṇḍāla caste; the All-Illumining family, the family of a single family, the family of diversity, the family that performs action, and the family from which all arises; the vajra lineage and the families of its group, the jewel lineage and the families of its group, the lotus lineage and the families of its group, the youthful lineage and the families of its group, and the elephant lineage and the families of its group.

"In the same way, the countless buddhas and bodhisattvas who appear everywhere and in every way are also only of the five families, by the power of karma and of engagement. But by the power of things as they are, everywhere and in every way, there is no sign of family. There is no convention. There is no aspect. There is no object. There is no engagement. There is no becoming a buddha. There is no prophecy. There is no finding of certainty. Everywhere and in every way, it is awakening itself. It is emptiness itself. It is the essence itself. It is suchness itself. It is genuineness itself. It is the sublime certainty itself. It is non-otherness itself. It is non-arising itself. It is non-cessation itself. It is suchness. It is unerringness itself. It is the nature of reality. It is the expanse of phenomena itself. Being inconceivable, it is unthinkability itself. It is the equality of the limit of reality."

So he prophesied. And because the sound of the supreme secret of suchness resounded from the emptiness of the suchness of glorious Vajrasattva, the holy beings, everywhere and in every way, found certainty in the very heart of awakening.

"O Lord of Laṅkā, the supreme secret, the suchness of the awakening mind, is this difficult, this profound, this vast, this unthinkable. Because it is inconceivable, finding certainty in it is just this difficult."

Then the Lord of Laṅkā asked again: "O Lord of Secrets, is this supreme secret, the suchness of the awakening mind, something with continuity, or is it something whose continuity is cut off? If it has continuity, then great wisdom would be subject to arising and ceasing. But if its continuity is cut off, then there would be no distinctions of wisdom upon wisdom, and no level of intention upon intention."

When he had asked this, the Lord of Secrets said: "O Lord of Laṅkā, the supreme secret, the suchness of the awakening mind, has continuity without arising or ceasing."

He asked: "Lord of Secrets, what is it that has continuity?"

He said: "Lord of Laṅkā, wisdom has continuity. Compassion has continuity. Enlightened activity has continuity. Qualities have continuity. Blessing has continuity. Because aspiration has continuity, it is uninterrupted."

He asked: "Lord of Secrets, why are these called continuity?"

He said: "Lord of Laṅkā, it is because they arise as the very nature of continuity."

He asked: "What is the nature of continuity?"

He said: "Lord of Laṅkā, that very nonexistence is the nature of continuity. That is to say:

The awakening mind that has continuity,
though causeless, appears to the gaze of imputation and clinging.
Within the great suffering of appearance and desire,
its greatness and qualities are unbroken in continuity.
The supreme secret, suchness itself,
which is ever possessed of continuity,
is, through those supreme lamps,
for those holy beings,
that very continuum within great suchness.
Apart from the dawning of the intention,
it is not something shown by words.
What is seen as causeless and without appearance
is primordially released, unfabricated, just as it is.
That very certainty is suchness itself:
appearing as nothing at all, free of analysis,
beyond thought, beyond all objects.

O Lord of Laṅkā, one should abide in every way in this supreme secret essence of all buddhas. Why is that? Because those swollen with doctrines of action strive for ten million eons with immeasurable hardships, yet do not reach the supreme; whereas those who have not reached the supreme will, through the meaning of nonaction, without doubt attain the supreme secret merely by an instant's, a moment's devotion to this secret. Therefore, Lord of Laṅkā, one should abide in this. One should not stray from it."

When he had said this, the Lord of Laṅkā and the others found the certainty of supreme understanding.

This concludes the fourteenth chapter, the Prophecy of the Holy Beings, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

From the Way of Going Forth in the Great Vehicle, this is the third part, teaching how it came about: the account of those who found certainty, a discussion of the wisdom of the Dharma seal.

Then once more the Lord of Laṅkā put a full question to the Lord of Secrets about the prophecy given earlier, the fourth of the twelve statements, which said, "It will also come about when ascertained as the very essence of awakening." He asked: "O Lord of Secrets, when you yourself told how the supreme secret of the Great Vehicle came about, you set down the words, 'It will also come about when ascertained as the very essence of awakening.' In what way is it ascertained, and how does it come about?"

He said: "Lord of Laṅkā, it is that the holy ones appear in every way as the very essence of awakening."

He said: "Lord of Secrets, when those holy beings who have fully found certainty abide in the essence of awakening, how do they appear in every way? If it is something that appears, it cannot be unarisen and unborn. If it is something that does not appear, continuous wisdom and continuous compassion cannot arise. How then should this be understood?"

When he had asked this, the Lord of Secrets said: "O Lord of Laṅkā, it is like this. The holy being is just as the essence of awakening is. Whatever is the essence of awakening is that very holy being, and whatever is the holy being is the very essence of awakening. A 'holy being' is not seen, and an 'essence of awakening' is not apprehended."

He asked: "If it is not seen and not apprehended, this contradicts what the holy ones have taught: 'Whoever sees that itself sees all phenomena; whoever sees all phenomena sees the essence of awakening,' and 'Whoever finds certainty in suchness has found certainty.' How then should this be understood?"

When he had asked this, he said: "O Lord of Laṅkā, it is like this:

Whatever form or appearance is seen is no substance found;
not seeing is that seeing, that very certainty.
Of the inexpressible awareness whose experience is found,
'seeing' and 'finding' are spoken as mere conventions.
On that great and utterly pure path itself,
the holy beings who have come forth
find full certainty in the great meaning,
and within the very state of nonapprehension,
when they neither stir nor conceive,
there is the essential nature of the nonabiding dharmakāya,
self-arisen wisdom, vast as space,
unmixed, fully perfect and clear,
suchness, unborn and unceasing,
beyond speech, thought, expression and concept,
the domain of each one's own awareness,
center and edge unapprehended, just as it is.
In the essence of awakening, the Mother of all,
unelaborated, it is vividly present in bliss."

Again the Lord of Laṅkā asked: "Lord of Secrets, for how long does that suchness, the essence of awakening, abide? What are its characteristics and its nature?"

He said: "Lord of Laṅkā, it is not fixed at all: not by seasons, months or half-months, by nights or days, by sessions or half-sessions, by instants, moments or brief moments, by what immediately follows, by suchness itself, by eons or by immeasurable eons. For it is without characteristics and without nature. It is nonapprehended. It is made familiar. It is definitively examined. It is fully experienced. It appears as anything whatsoever. It is ascertained as being. It arises everywhere. It gathers all. It pervades the whole. It is spontaneously born. It is utterly pure. It is established nowhere at all. Since it is without pervading and without not pervading, without high and low, without great and small, without good and bad, without wide and narrow, without cause and effect, without male and female, without family lineage, without form, without karma and its ripening, without outer and inner, without birth, without transference or change, without darkness and light, without before and after, without edge and center, without self and other, without effort and accomplishment, without adopting and rejecting, naturally unfabricated, spontaneously luminous, uncorrupted, unarisen and unproduced, why, Lord of Laṅkā, do you ask what its characteristics and its nature are? The essence of awakening transcends appearance and nonappearance, and its characteristics and nature are not apprehended.

Thus, Lord of Laṅkā, those holy beings who abide in the essence of awakening and do not apprehend are wedded to that supreme suchness, Vajrasattva, emptiness, and therefore this comes forth from it: 'O holy beings, do you see the essence of awakening?' And from the holy beings, from that suchness of the essence of awakening, this comes forth: 'O supreme spiritual friend, we see the essence of awakening without seeing it.'

He declared: 'How do you see without seeing?'

They replied: 'Not seeing anything as the essence of awakening, we see all as the essence of awakening.'

He declared: 'What is called the essence of awakening?'

They replied: 'Supreme spiritual friend, the essence of awakening is the name for the secret supreme intention of all buddhas.'

He declared: 'Holy beings, what is called the supreme intention?'

They replied: 'It is the suchness that is the nature of all.'"

He declared: "Why is the nature of all called secret mantra?"

They replied: "Because whatever is the nature of all is seen by no one, realized by no one, has never become anyone's domain, cannot be spoken, transcends expression, cannot be thought, is inconceivable, and is hard to examine and hard to teach."

He declared: "Holy beings, do you see the nature of all?"

They replied: "Supreme spiritual friend, we do not see the nature of all."

He declared: "Why do you not see it?"

They replied: "Because it is not apprehended."

He declared: "Holy beings, do you then not see the nature of all?"

They replied: "Supreme spiritual friend, we do see it."

He declared: "Holy beings, in what way do you see it?"

They replied: "Whatever is the nature of all, unfabricated, uncorrupted, unarisen, unborn, suchness just as it is, we see as the maṇḍala of what is primordially just as it is."

He declared: "Do you see the Buddha?"

They replied: "We do not see him."

He declared: "Why do you not see him?"

They replied: "Because he is not apprehended."

He declared: "Do you then not see him?"

They replied: "We do see him."

He declared: "In what way do you see him?"

They replied: "Because all are awakened as suchness and pervade everything, we see the maṇḍala of the spontaneously accomplished nature."

He declared: "Do you see all minds?"

They replied: "We do not see them."

He declared: "Why do you not see them?"

They replied: "Because they are not apprehended."

He declared: "Do you then not see them?"

They replied: "We do see them."

He declared: "In what way do you see them?"

They replied: "We see all as the maṇḍala of the root, the awakening mind."

He declared: "Holy beings, do you wish for the essence of awakening?"

They replied: "Supreme spiritual friend, it is already fully ripened."

He declared: "Why do you not wish for it?"

They replied: "Because what is called a holy being, and sentient beings, what are called sentient beings, have never moved from the essence of awakening. All, too, have never moved from suchness."

He declared: "Holy beings, where do you seek the essence of awakening?"

They replied: "We do not seek it anywhere at all."

He declared: "Why do you not seek it?"

They replied: "Because all is the very essence of awakening, and it abides nowhere."

He declared: "Do the holy beings wish to journey through all the buddha fields?"

They replied: "We do not wish to journey through them."

He declared: "Why do you not journey through them?"

They replied: "Because all the classes of sentient beings without exception are themselves the utterly pure buddha field."

He declared: "Holy beings, do you abide unmoving in nonconceptuality?"

They replied: "We do not enter nonconceptuality."

He declared: "Why do you not enter it?"

They replied: "Because, being ourselves nonconceptual, we have no need to enter nonconceptuality only now. If we were not nonconceptual from the very beginning, then we might enter nonconceptuality; but all is nonconceptual from the very beginning."

He declared: "Holy beings, do you wish great compassion to arise?"

They replied: "We do not wish it to arise."

He declared: "Why do you not wish it?"

They replied: "We are wholly unmoving."

He declared: "Why are you unmoving?"

"Because all is nothing but buddhahood itself, and so no object of compassion is apprehended."

He declared: "Holy beings, do you act for the benefit of sentient beings?"

They replied: "We do not act."

He declared: "Why do you not act?"

They replied: "Because all is from the very beginning nothing but suchness."

He declared: "Holy beings, do you wish to accomplish the spontaneously present dharmakāya?"

They replied: "We do not seek to accomplish it."

He declared: "Why do you not wish to accomplish it?"

They replied: "Because what is accomplished arises from what is compounded."

He declared: "Do the holy beings wish to make the full journey?"

They replied: "We do not make the full journey."

He declared: "Why do you not make the journey?"

They replied: "Because all, as buddhas, have already gone the whole way of the path."

He declared: "Holy beings, by what example is that essence of awakening shown?"

They replied: "Supreme spiritual friend, there is no example that matches it; it cannot be compared by any example at all."

He declared: "Can it not be indicated by an example?"

They replied: "If one takes examples that match it slightly, it is like space, the sun, a precious jewel, precious gold, a lamp, the elixir that transforms poison, the elixir that transforms metal into gold, beryl, the great ocean, the heart of stone, what overpowers by its prowess, what enters into objects, precious gold ore, and what arouses delight."

He declared: "Holy beings, how is that essence of awakening known by the names that indicate it?"

They replied: "Supreme spiritual friend, names are mere designations; there is nothing fixed in a name."

He declared: "Can it not be indicated by a name?"

They replied: "If one indicates it in terms of expression, out of what is wholly unapprehended, the various names that are conceived are known and held to according to however they are differently imputed. It is widely known as the essence of awakening, thatness, reality, suchness, the just-as-it-is, the unerring, the unfabricated, the not-other, dharmatā, the dharmadhātu, the inconceivable, the limit of reality, secret mantra, dhāraṇī-mantra, vidyā-mantra, and the Great Vehicle."

He declared: "Holy beings, how does that essence of awakening appear?"

They replied: "It is impossible for that essence of awakening to appear even for an instant."

He declared: "Does it then not appear anywhere at all?"

They replied: "However it is looked at, so it appears."

He declared: "How does it fully appear?"

They replied: "It is like this:
Because it pervades everything, it is without appearance.
Hidden nowhere, it is unseen.
Never separated from it from the very beginning, one does not recognize it.
Examined, it is not seen, yet it appears as anything at all.
Though it appears in variety, it is without substance.
Though one wanders for eons, it is never lost or parted from.
Though its doors are various, it is without change.
Without any particle, it pervades all that appears and exists.
From emptiness itself, variety arises.
Without any actual basis, it is imputed and clung to as anything at all.
Without striving or ground, whatever is accomplished is accomplished.
Without form or mark, it appears as whatever one looks at.
Being nothing whatsoever, it is aware as anything at all.
All-pervading and all-enveloping, it is one and limitless.
Free of all, it is singular, yet nothing is abandoned.
Without outer or inner, it is inner throughout.

Though it arises endlessly, it is inexhaustible.
Neither grasped nor examined, it is clung to wherever one is.
Without anything material, it is ever falling.
Without being lifted, it travels through the sky.
Without limbs, it journeys through the grounds.
Never moving anywhere, it has reached everywhere.
Without stirring, it is unobstructed and swift.
Though elaborated as distinct, it never moves from the one.
Never moving from the one, it appears as variety.
Because all enter by it, it is the path.
Because nothing is abandoned, it is saṃsāra.
Because of the confusion of not knowing, it is sentient beings.
Because it ferries across the four rivers, it is the boat.
Because it moves onward, it is the journey.
Because it has no reference point, it is awakening.
Because it has various aspects, it is mind.
Because it is skilled in all, it is wisdom.
Because all is clear, it is samādhi.
Because it arrives without journeying, it is the abode.
Because it has no equal, it is the highest.
Because it is wondrous, it is the supreme.
Because it is utterly exalted, it is the finest.
Because nothing has power over it and there is no antidote to it, it is unsurpassed.
Because its depth cannot be found, it is profound.
Because it is immeasurable, it is secret.
Because it is subtle, it is not evident.
Because all arises from it, it is Dharma.
Because it does not change, it is suchness.
Because it is the very station, it is the bodhisattva.
Because the buddhas pervade it continuously, it is the Buddha.
Because it has become the ground, it is the root.
Because it gives birth to all, it is the Mother.
Because it comes from her, it is the Father.
Because it holds the lineage, it is the Son.
Because it is self-arisen, it is the Sovereign Lord.
Because it is suchness, it is the great lineage.
Because it is hard to transgress, it is the great commitment.
Because it sustains life, it is the life force.
Because it knows no exhaustion, it is the great treasure.
Because it fulfills desires, it is wealth.
Because with continuous compassion it does the work of all, it is the servant.
Because it is majestic, it is body.
Because utterance and expression arise from it, it is speech.
Because it knows all, it is mind.
Because every wish arises from it, it is qualities.
Because it performs deeds, it is enlightened activity.
Because it goes toward objects, it is desire.
Because it enjoys suchness itself, it is the great enjoyment.
Because it has become the cause, it is the great requisite.
Because the intention is complete, it is the great gathering.
Because it is without effortful action, it is the accomplishment of all aims.
Because it conjures variety, it is miracle.
Because it has arisen as a sign, it is mudrā.
Because it is not sought elsewhere, it is approach.
Because it arises from oneself, it is close approach.
Because it is the essence, it is accomplishment.
Because it is the fruit, it is great accomplishment.

He declared: "Holy beings, how do you accomplish that essence of awakening?"

They replied: "It cannot be accomplished."

He declared: "How is it that it cannot be accomplished?"

They replied: "Because it is accomplished from the very beginning."

He declared: "How is it accomplished from the very beginning?"

They replied: "Because it does not arise from elsewhere, arises from itself, is without effortful action, and is self-arisen, it is ascertained as experienced from the very beginning."

Thus, as the sound came forth in this way from emptiness, suchness is ascertained as suchness, and in every respect there is nothing anywhere apart from the very essence of awakening.

Lord of Laṅkā, in this way, when the holy beings who are certain as the very essence of awakening rest, peaceful and vividly present, as nonconceptuality itself within the unmoving state, then, at that time, the sun of wisdom that explains the intention spreads through the sky of dharmatā and explains all as the very certainty of suchness.

Then, at that time, when, as the cause for the arising of secret mantra, there had appeared special disciples, burners of the three worlds, of wrathful manner, and the time for taming these disciples had surely come, all the buddhas of every direction and time gathered where the essence of awakening is and roused the holy beings: some they roused to great awakening, some to great miracles, some to complete the rites, some through samādhi, and some through the awareness of liberation. Thereby they rose from where emptiness is, and the complete enjoyment and the array of emanations appeared exactly as in the Complete Array of the Lamp, the magical display of Vajrasattva's certainty of suchness.

It is like this: buddhas, purely conceived, distinct beyond all telling, in distinct buddha fields; distinct universal monarchs; complete enjoyment in the distinct heavens of the Thirty-Three; regents and Dharma kings; emanations as distinct Śakras (Indra) and gurus of the gods; distinct miracles appearing at distinct final ages; secret bodies appearing distinctly in the distinct wombs of mothers of uṣṇīṣa wheel-turning monarchs; distinct emanated lamps appearing in distinct buddha fields; the mind unmoving in the essence of awakening; distinct bodies in distinct worlds entering the absorption of meditative concentration and bringing their deeds to completion; going to distinct vajra seats, taming distinct māras, fully turning the wheel, and attaining full and perfect awakening: only this.

When he had said this, the Lord of Laṅkā and the other beings of inferior bodies became certain of the essence of awakening, the supreme secret, as suchness.

This concludes the fifteenth chapter, Certainty in the Supreme Secret as Suchness, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

From the Way of Going Forth in the Great Vehicle, this is the fourth part, teaching how it came about: the account of certainty as the very essence of awakening, a discussion of the wisdom of the Dharma seal.

Here begins the third fascicle.

Then the Lord of Laṅkā put a full question to the Lord of Secrets about the fifth of the twelve statements given earlier, which said, "It also came about at the Explanation of the Intention." He asked: "O Lord of Secrets, when you yourself recounted the prophecy teaching how the supreme secret of the Great Vehicle came about, you set down the words, 'It also came about at the Explanation of the Intention.' In what way is such an Explanation of the Intention known?"

He said: "Lord of Laṅkā, it refers to the very time and moment when those who are certain as holy beings held the essence of awakening in their intention."

He said: "Lord of Secrets, how do those who are certain as truly holy beings hold the essence of awakening in their intention? Of what are those words a designation?"

He said: "Lord of Laṅkā, 'essence' refers to the root. 'Awakening' refers to the nature. 'Intention' refers to dharmatā. 'Definitive' refers to the unerring. 'Explanation' refers to wisdom. These are designations for the just-as-it-is and the thus.

He asked: "Lord of Secrets, how is that wisdom explained?"

He said: "Lord of Laṅkā, it is explained as unmixed and fully perfect."

He asked: "Lord of Secrets, how should that be understood?"

He said: "Lord of Laṅkā, it is calm abiding and insight themselves. Everything is gathered into four births and explained as four births, as follows. Whatever the Tathāgata does, his intention, his speech and all his deeds are intended toward something; and so he intends all phenomena as gathered into four miracles. Lord of Laṅkā, what are the four? When the miracle of birth arises with respect to phenomena that are unborn, there is the miracle born from the magical power of karma, the miracle born from the magical power of meditative concentration, the miracle born from the magical power of dharmatā, and the miracle born from the magical power of compassion.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, the miracle born from the magical power of karma is everything that appears as the four kinds of birth. Lord of Laṅkā, what is the miracle born from the magical power of meditative concentration? It is everything born from the four alternatives of meditative concentration. Lord of Laṅkā, what is the miracle born from the magical power of dharmatā? It is everything that appears as the four miracles of nature. Lord of Laṅkā, what is the miracle born from the magical power of compassion? It is what appears as four miracles: everything that appears as the four miracles of magical power, of the appearance of the Dharma, of full declaration, and of subsequent instruction.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, the miracle of magical power is the miracle of making something appear and the miracle of transforming into something. Lord of Laṅkā, the miracle of making something appear is the miracle intended to tame by indicating to whom, the miracle intended to tame by indicating through what, the miracle intended to tame by that into which it has changed, the miracle intended to tame by vehicles, and the miracle intended to tame by how it is beautiful.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, the miracle intended to tame by indicating to whom is the intention to tame by the unfabricated and the intention to tame by transformation. Lord of Laṅkā, of these, the intention to tame by the unfabricated is like a conch, like a white lily, like the moon, and like the sun. Lord of Laṅkā, the intention to tame by transformation is like beryl, like kaṅkari, like lac dye, like refined gold, and like many colors.

Lord of Laṅkā, the miracle intended to tame by indicating through what is what has arisen, what appears, and how it changes. Lord of Laṅkā, of these, what has arisen is intended as two or six. Likewise, what appears is also intended as that. Lord of Laṅkā, how it changes is the unfabricated, the untransgressed, the unfixed, and the connected. Lord of Laṅkā, the unfabricated is the unborn and the unceasing. Of these, what is the unceasing? It is object, indication, sign, and close imputation. Lord of Laṅkā, the untransgressed is what is complete, what is enacted, and what does not lean. Lord of Laṅkā, what is the unfixed? It is appearing as endlessly distinct by the power of taming. Lord of Laṅkā, what is the connected? It is the intention of connection as the holy, and the intention of connection through action.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the miracle intended to tame by that into which it has changed? It is means, place, and manner. Lord of Laṅkā, what is means? It is the intention toward nature and the intention toward distinction. Lord of Laṅkā, what is place? Without outer or inner, it has become inner throughout. Lord of Laṅkā, what is manner? It is the manner of sitting, the manner of rising, the manner of going, the manner of gazing, the supple manner, the lithe manner, the entwining manner, the swaying manner, the youthful manner, the clear manner, the radiant manner, the manner of massive beauty, the manner of blazing splendor, the flashing manner, the contemplative manner, the steady manner, the relying manner, the soaring manner, the enchanting manner, the glittering manner, the soft manner, the vividly present manner, and the lucid manner.

Lord of Laṅkā, the miracle intended to tame by vehicles is turning the wheel of Dharma, journeying through the distinctions of the grounds, and the conquering greatness of qualities.

Lord of Laṅkā, the miracle intended to tame by how it is beautiful is this: because it is beautified by discrimination of phenomena, joy, pliancy, samādhi, mindfulness, equanimity, and diligence, it is intended as arrayed with great ornaments. Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, because it swells with body, speech, mind, qualities, and enlightened activity, it is intended as majestic in great attire. Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, because it is beautified by the flowers of the major marks and adorned with the fruits of the minor marks, it is fully ornamented by the host of heirs. Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, because it is coiled with samādhi, effort, speech, thought, the scope of action, view, livelihood, and mindfulness, it is intended as blazing with an auspicious coil.

Lord of Laṅkā, these are called the miracle of making something appear. In suchness they are each unmixed, and they are fully explained as one."

When the Lord of Secrets had said this, the Lord of Laṅkā and the other beings of inferior bodies came to see all phenomena as nothing but the appearance of miracles.

Then the Lord of Secrets spoke again: "Lord of Laṅkā, the miracle of transforming into something is the ten mountains of conceptuality.

What are the ten? They are the conception of application, the conception of clinging, the conception of the main part, the conception of generation, the conception of conditions, the conception of action, the conception of signs, the conception of manifestation, the conception of conduct, and the conception of marks.

Lord of Laṅkā, from these ten root conceptions come ninety, or eighty-one, mountains of conceptuality. Because they cut across the supreme path of yoga and make it hard to traverse, one should strive to purify them completely.

Lord of Laṅkā, what are the nine? They are the conception of names, the conception of signs, the conception of symbols, the conception of conventions, the conception of existence, the conception of nonexistence, the conception of doubt, the conception that settles certainty, and the conception of analysis. Because these become the cause of turning into ten kinds each, they are the mountains of ten times nine.

Lord of Laṅkā, what are the eighteen and the one? They are these: three in threes of three; four in threes of five; five in ones of four; six in ones of six; seven in ones of three; eight in twos of five and ones of six; nine in ones of twelve; and ten in ones of seven. Thus there are eighteen in general, and what is examined is the one conception.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, what is the conception of application? It is the nonconceptual, the unconfused, and the unobstructing. Lord of Laṅkā, what is the conception of clinging? It is the unapprehended, the unestablished, and the unborn. Lord of Laṅkā, what is the conception of the main part? It is being of one cause, one conduct, and one nature. Lord of Laṅkā, what is the conception of generation? It is what has changed, into what it has changed, for what reason it has changed, and by what it has changed.

Lord of Laṅkā, what has changed is this: what has changed naturally, what has changed by force, what is accompanied by change, and what is fabricated as change. Lord of Laṅkā, into what it has changed is this: change into objects, change into places, the action of change, and the result of change. Lord of Laṅkā, for what reason it has changed is this: change for the sake of the cause, and change for the sake of the result. Lord of Laṅkā, by what it has changed is this: change by the cause, change by the place, change by means, change by action, and change by the result.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the conception of conditions? It is crushing, destroying, devouring, and feeding.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the conception of action? It is the action of gathering, the action of bliss, the action of appearance, the limitless action, the unobstructed action, and the unsupported action.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the conception of signs? It is the untransgressed, play, and greatness. Lord of Laṅkā, of these, what is the untransgressed? It is not transgressing the disciples, not transgressing the nature, not transgressing wisdom, not transgressing means, not transgressing the commitment, not transgressing compassion, not transgressing the holy, not transgressing enlightened activity, not transgressing primordial wisdom, not transgressing dharmatā, and not transgressing the intention.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is play? It is accomplishment and deeds. What is accomplishment? It is the unborn, the unconfused, the unmoving, the unceasing, the unattached, the unbound, the unsupported, the unobstructed, the all-inclusive, and the untraversed. Lord of Laṅkā, what are deeds? They are the deed of gazing upon disciples, the deed of sending forth emanations, the deed of hearing whatever is asked, the deed of being the supreme protector and refuge, the deed of exhausting the great poisons, the deed of teaching the supreme vehicle, the deed of cutting off the four kinds of birth, the deed of drawing out the six continua, the deed of completing the six limits, and the deed of liberating by taking on actions.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is greatness? It is the greatness that has no antidote, the greatness that has no rival, the greatness of special exaltation, the greatness of sole might, the greatness of overwhelming waves, the wondrous greatness, the greatness undefeated anywhere, the greatness of complete dominion, the greatness victorious over all, and the greatness that never wanes.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the conception of manifestation? It is the grandeur of body manifesting in tens of millions and millions, the grandeur of speech manifesting teachings like an ocean, the grandeur of mind manifesting as the sky of dharmatā, and the grandeur of power manifesting like the expanse of space.

Lord of Laṅkā, these are completed by sixteen modes. What are they? They are the mode like a mountain, just as it appears; the mode like a dance, changing into anything at all; the mode of overpowering, extraordinary; the mode of great majesty, swaggering in the fierceness of its strength; the mode of the mouth, inhaling wherever it may be; the mode of the tongue, changing into anything at all; the mode of the teeth, cutting through completely; the mode of thunder resounding, proclaiming understanding; the mode of the intention, like the hosts of stars in the sky; the mode of enlightened activity, like the waves of the ocean; the mode of qualities, like the plants and trees of the earth; the mode of compassion, like a garland of dance; the mode of the charnel ground, the distinction of place, where things arise and are gathered in; the mode of intimidation, the distinction of subjugation; the mode of the unsurpassed, the distinction of field; and the mode accomplished all at once and unsupported, the distinction of conduct.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the consideration of conduct? It is as follows: the splendor of body, like the form of a hundred thousand lions; the splendor of speech, like a thousand dragons roaring; the splendor of mind, like the great expanse of the sky; the splendor of qualities, like a precious jewel; and the splendor of enlightened activity, like a great maṇḍala. Lord of Laṅkā, these are complete with twelve splendors. And what are they? They are as follows: the splendor of a great body with robust limbs; the splendor of great numbers suited to conditions; the splendor of being seen as foremost and worthy of honor; the splendor of being distinguished and pleasing to the mind; the splendor of a great resonance that fills the three-thousandfold world system; the splendor of swift words that cut through doubts; the splendor of teaching as though to each one alone; the splendor of seeing special qualities; the splendor of vastness, free of hope and fear; the splendor of clarity that abandons the two extremes; the splendor of compassion that fulfills the sacred commitment; the splendor of taming by undeluded means; the splendor of never running out for those in want; the splendor of displaying, from one, whatever is wished for; the splendor of turning harmful deeds into benefit; the splendor of skillful means unstained by faults; the splendor of fearless, confident play; the splendor of never forgetting through long ages in saṃsāra; the splendor of continually acting as protector and refuge; and the splendor of spontaneously accomplishing the intended aim.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the consideration of signs? It is as follows: the sign that the king of Dharma has attained the great power that is free of fear; the sign of great, confident play; the sign of being unafraid of any army; the sign of having attained lasting sovereignty; the sign of overpowering all others by strength; the sign of moving without abiding through miraculous power; and the sign of possessing an inexhaustible treasury. Lord of Laṅkā, the sign of having attained great dominion is this: the complete perfection of the ten masteries. What are the ten? Mastery over birth, over requisites, over inclination, over miraculous power, over the Dharma, over wisdom, over mind, over life, over karma, and over aspiration.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the sign of great, confident play? It is as follows: play in the four seals, that is, play in the equality of body, the drops of mind, the commentary of speech, and the waves of enlightened activity.

Lord of Laṅkā, the sign of being unafraid of any army is as follows: the mode of the alluring, the mode of the heroic, the mode of the terrifying, the mode of the scornful, the mode of laughter, the mode of compassion, the mode of the fierce, the mode of the wondrous, and the mode of the peaceful.

Lord of Laṅkā, the sign of having attained lasting sovereignty is as follows: the intelligence of loving-kindness, the counsel of compassion, the method of joy, and the sūtra of equanimity.

Lord of Laṅkā, the sign of overpowering all others by strength is as follows: the power of compassion, the power of the nature of reality, the power of nondifferentiation, the power of accomplishment, the power of not abandoning, the power of being unmoved, the power of aspiration, the power of emanation, the power of not giving up, and the power of attachment.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the sign of moving without abiding through miraculous power? It is as follows: the miraculous power of the nature of reality, the miraculous power of wisdom, the miraculous power of compassion, and the miraculous power of emanation.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the sign of possessing an inexhaustible treasury? It is as follows: fulfilling wishes, arising inexhaustibly, dispelling darkness, and never being interrupted.

Lord of Laṅkā, these are called the miracle of magical power. Lord of Laṅkā, all the infinite considerations, however many there are, gathered within the miracle of magical power, are fully explained as one, each distinct and unmixed.

When he had said this, the Lord of Laṅkā and the other bodhisattvas of inferior bodies saw all phenomena appearing as nothing but miracles of magical power.

This concludes the sixteenth chapter, the teaching of the miracle of magical power, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then again the Lord of Secrets called to the Lord of Laṅkā and said: "O Lord of Laṅkā, what is the miracle of the appearance of the Dharma? It is as follows. When the miracle was shown by which the Dharma definitely appears, through whomever and wherever, in each of those places all those world systems shook, shook strongly, shook strongly everywhere; moved, moved strongly, moved strongly everywhere; rocked, rocked strongly, rocked strongly everywhere; roared, roared strongly, roared strongly everywhere; quivered, quivered strongly, quivered strongly everywhere. When the edges rose, the center sank; when the center rose, the edges sank; when the east rose, the west sank; when the west rose, the east sank; when the south rose, the north sank; and when the north rose, the south sank. All outer and inner darkness was purified, and endless beings were filled with delight and wonder. The maṇḍala of the mind became luminous, and they tasted the joy of the nature of reality of the Great Vehicle. Each and every thing appeared as the sacred nature of reality, as follows:

In worlds as infinite as particles of dust,
Emanations infinite as dust were sent forth;
Darkness dispelled, all things were purified,
And all appeared, uncontrived, just as it is.
From the meaning of emanations numerous as dust
Come the gateways where the nature of reality surely appears;
What appears as fourteen from two
Appears clearly as clusters of letters and syllables.

Lord of Laṅkā, it is as follows: a is the gateway illuminating the very expanse of phenomena. hūṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of wisdom. hūṃ is the gateway fully illuminating the nature of reality of the unmoving. oṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of appearing as anything whatsoever. svā is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the arising of variety. oṃḥ is the gateway illuminating the infinite nature of reality. hā is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of Amoghasiddhi. mūṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality beyond expression. lāṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality without arising or ceasing. rmāṃ is the gateway illuminating the inexhaustible nature of reality. phyāṃ is the gateway illuminating the faultless nature of reality. tāṃ is the gateway illuminating the unobstructed nature of reality. hrīṃḥ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality that holds speech. trāṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality that opens space. kṣa is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of seeing.

jrīṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of destroying. hūṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the root, the mind of awakening. ṭāṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of arising as anything whatsoever. trīṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of proclaiming sound. a is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality that is not fixed as anything at all. mai is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of great loving-kindness. thlhīṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of great compassion. hūṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of great joy. mūṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of great equanimity. du is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the miracle of meditative absorption. pu is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the miracle of empowerment. vaṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the miracle of blessing. hoḥ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the miracle of offering. ya is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the liberation of emptiness. tha is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the liberation of signlessness. hriḥ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the liberation of wishlessness. hūṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the liberation of skill in means. jaḥ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of drawing forth the meaning. hūṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of expressing the meaning. vaṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of practicing the meaning. hoḥ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of according with the meaning. kṛṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of awakening. truṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the powers. sruṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the faculties. pra is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of miraculous power. kṣaṃ is the gateway illuminating the defining characteristic of abandonment. ye is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of mindfulness. bruṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the empty. e is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the wrathful. vaṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the peaceful. ma is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of expanding to the limits. ya is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of gathering in. śva is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of arising outwardly and inwardly. dha is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the source of phenomena. ma is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of mindfulness. hā is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of calling. pra is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of sending forth. kṣaḥ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of final exhaustion. jñā is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of knowing. te is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the cause. saṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the result. thaṃ is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the condition. ska is the gateway illuminating the nature of reality of the aggregates. kra
