Such a one is his own obstructor.

If you ask what that is like,
A yogin called Mati,
Holy in understanding, came
To where the yogin called Abhaya
Practiced meaningless contrivance.
Out of malice toward that patient one,
Abhaya slandered him and spoke words of reproach;
Thereby Abhaya fell into the Avīci hell.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.

Moreover, Lord of Secrets, listen:
Those who, parted from the target of the view,
Are not clear about the path of the true Middle Way,
And practice relying on mere generic objects,
Are a host of blind yogins.

If you ask what that is like,
A king called Holder of Great Clarity
Followed the secret yoga;
But lacking the eye of the view of wisdom,
He took up the mere name and manner of yoga,
And by practicing its ways in the wake of the sounds he heard,
He fell into the realm of the darkness of delusion.
Such ways will arise: do not practice them.

These and endless others, supreme ones,
All fall to being travelers on the path of saṃsāra.
Therefore this heart of the secret
Is taught by the victors to have great pitfalls.
Therefore skill in means
And holy understanding are most precious.
The yogin endowed with skill in means and understanding
Is certainly excellent.

This account of the great adepts
Who came in times long past
Is the domain of holy beings.
Whoever hears the proclamation of their qualities
Attains happiness even by their mere names,
And by recollecting them is protected from the lower realms.

If you ask what that is like,
In times long ago, when this
Most secret heart had not yet appeared,
In the place renowned as the divine abode of the Thirty-Three,
There was a son of the gods, Endowed with Light.
When the time of impermanence and death befell him,
And the omens that come beforehand appeared,
He came to perceive unease.
Turning his attention to what was to come,
He saw with his own clairvoyance
That he would take the body of a stupid creature
That lives on unclean food.
With the perception of utter terror
He brought to mind an object of refuge.
Though nothing else came to mind there,
The name of the heart of the secret of the Great Vehicle,
Just as it was renowned in the path of words,
He had heard, and by bringing it to mind,
He turned back from going to the lower realms
And was placed in the abode of great bliss.
Such ways will arise: practice in the same way.

Moreover, Lord of Secrets, listen:
If one is thoroughly certain about the secret of the Great Vehicle,
Then even if violent karmic ripening hurls one somewhere,
Like a ball that is struck,
One rebounds, and by the power of the meaning does not stay there.
Why? Because all is emptiness:
The places of suffering too,
Together with their beings, are empty,
And so one does not come to touch and experience them.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
A yogin who held to conceptual focus,
Hearing the words of the profound Great Vehicle,
Was utterly afraid and terrified;
He disparaged it, turned his back, and his heart burst.
Yet without experiencing a fall into the Raurava hell,
He emerged into the happy realms, the state of liberation.
Know that such ways have come about.

Whoever delights in this secret of the victors
And gives rise to longing for it
Will, with no obstacles arising from others,
Swiftly attain whatever he aspires to.

If you ask what that is like,
It is like what came about in times past:
A brahmin called Endowed with Love
Saw this great secret, the heart of the secret,
And was drawn to it, and looked for it everywhere.
But he did not find it,
And remaining where he was,
He wept bitterly and cried out in grief:
"Ah, how wretched my karma is!
From my birth until I have grown old
I have sought the meaning of the true secret;
That I have not seen it, how evil my karma is!
How will I see it now?
It is not something found by searching;
Who is there to teach me?"
When he had said this, weeping loudly,
A voice came forth from the realm of space:
"You, endowed with fortune, of noble lineage,
Because your mind is so steadfast in certainty,
You are one who has a share in the meaning;
All the victors of the three times
Bestow their blessing upon you.
Therefore, without letting your aspiration slacken,
You must be skilled and diligent
In the means of meeting the meaning.
Once you have first cast off your material body,
If you look with certainty at the essential meaning,
You will see it immediately."
When he heard this prophecy,
His aspiration was greatly stirred by joy,
And looking just as he had been taught,
He became a son of the holders of the great family.
Such ways will arise: practice with aspiration.

Lord of Secrets, it is like this:
The fortunate who see it enter here,
And everywhere it is the same:
Like the finial at the top of a banner,
It never wholly declines.

And how was that?
One called King of Virtue,
Together with a hundred thousand young gods,
Toward the yogin of the secret essence,
The precious Endowed with Light, the Crown-Holder,
Was stirred to virtue with deep faith,
And taking many divine substances and requisites,
They went into the presence of that holy one.
With joy they took up the way of aspiration
And entered the meaning of the secret essence.
They obtained the four great miracles:
Traveling to every abode of the gods,
Emanating the great elements from the body,
Lifting mountains and hurling them,
And they obtained abiding in a long life.
Like the finial at the top of a victory banner,
The nature of reality of those who saw it
Did not decline for an eon.
Such ways have arisen: enter into it.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, it is like this:
By seeing, this Dharma is wholly pure.
Having found the eye of the lamp of wisdom,
One finds the path of those who possess the unsurpassed essence
And enters upon that path.
And how was that?
In times past it happened before:
One who had gained full certainty in the secret Great Vehicle,
Perfect in spontaneously present great dynamism,
Attained the very ground of a holder of the great family.
Such ways have arisen: see it.

Furthermore, holder of the great secret, listen:
Those who with joy rejoice
In the fierce rites of the Great Vehicle
Are just like those who perform them;
In the results there is no higher or lower.
And how was that?
In times past it happened before:
King Mighty Lord Endowed with Light,
Together with his retinue of yogins,
Entered the profound meaning of the Great Vehicle.
The blessings of the secret of secret mantra
And many wondrous miracles
At first became renowned everywhere.
Apart from them, there was a yogin,
A brahmin monk,
Who, giving rise to a mind of great joy,
Requested the holy and definitive training.
Because it is not the domain of the childish,
The great secret was taught in secret.
The brahmin, never turning back,
Dedicated his requisites to that one and applied them purely.
Toward the land of that holy being,
In his mind he voiced his aspiration in that direction.
Through the blessing of devotion to this,
The holy one certain in the essence
And the holy one who rejoiced, these two,
Became equal in their attainment.
Thus, without hardship and of great meaning,
Is the holder of the great family skilled in means.
Such ways have arisen: become skilled in them.

Furthermore, holder of secrets, it is like this:
Because the inconceivable victors,
Who pervade all with their minds, see this and know this,
One becomes a spiritual friend to the saṃsāra of the three realms of existence,
A holy lord of secrets.
And how was that?
In times past it happened before:
A householder called Bright Gift,
By abiding in this secret Great Vehicle,
Saw his own very secret,
Found certainty in the secret of the great secret,
And definitely saw the secret of all.
It is the secret of all phenomena;
From that, that arose, countless hundreds of thousands.
Having become a king of Dharma,
He became renowned in all buddha fields
As the Lion of All Speakers.
Such ways have arisen: train in them.

Furthermore, holder of secrets, Tamer of the Harmful, listen:
Compared with one who has come to understand
A single path of words of the secret Great Vehicle,
To spread great collections of offerings
Before inconceivable śrāvakas
And endless victors together with their heirs
Does not equal even a hundred-thousandth part.
Why is that? Because knowing this Great Vehicle
Is the supreme way of pleasing the victors.
The victors who pervade all of space
And the secret essence of the Great Vehicle, these two,
Are not separate; they are not different.
Therefore, by pleasing a single victor,
One pleases all the victors.
Body, speech, and mind are nothing but one;
It is only through differences in the capacities of those to be trained
That they are shown in inconceivable ways.
And how was that?
In times past it happened before:
In a fortunate and wholly pure family
There were two, called Endowed with Virtue and Endowed with Clarity.
Each was born according to where his inclination lay,
And here they made a pact with each other.
One practiced the lesser vehicle
And carried out the great work of ritual activity.
The other, from vast and certain understanding
Of the meaning of the secret Great Vehicle,
Endowed with Clarity, riding the Great Vehicle,
Was fully awakened as a single one of the great family,
While Endowed with Virtue, who performed ritual activity,
Was awakened only after three countless eons.
Therefore, in the secret Great Vehicle,
Such ways have arisen: practice properly.

Furthermore, lord, holder of secrets, listen:
Whoever always explains this secret
Obtains, like the constant great qualities
Of King Akṣobhya and the others,
Together with the dhāraṇī of meaning.
And how was that?
The householder called Agnidatta,
Together with a retinue of a hundred,
By always abiding in this Great Vehicle,
Attained, as one holder of the great family,
A state like that of the buddha King Akṣobhya
Together with his retinue.
Such ways have arisen: practice purely.

Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, it is like this:
Whoever, having realized those who definitely accomplish
For the sake of the vajra of body, speech, and mind,
Speaks of them,
Through habitual expression and aspiration
Becomes a king of Dharma
And becomes the very crown of qualities.
And how was that?
In times past it happened before:
The sage and yogin Kalya
Spoke praise of the secret Great Vehicle
To many different companies of sages.
Because he set out its meanings definitively,
Many attained the state of holders of the great family.
Whoever speaks praise of it there,
Lord of Secrets, becomes a king of Dharma.
Therefore, speak praise of this secret Great Vehicle to all.
By that, my intention is attained.
The heart commitment of all the victors, too,
Is the secret essence of the Great Vehicle.
If any yogin endowed with intelligence,
For an instant, a moment, a brief while,
Settles the great secret with certainty in secret,
All the victors too are fully certain
In the great secret of the holders of the family, in secret.
Lord of Secrets, thus it is:
Whatever the secret essence of the great secret,
The accounts of those definitively good
And the accounts of those definitively mistaken
Are the renowned lineage of what happened before.
Thus spoke the supreme secret itself.

When the Lord of Secrets had thus related how the tales of former times were handed down and became renowned, the doubts of the Lord of Laṅkā and the other beings of lesser body who had manifestly appeared, about what they had not been certain of, were completely cut off.

This concludes the thirty-eighth chapter of the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, the full relating of the tales of former events. From the Way of the Definite Emergence of the Great Vehicle: the Dharma seal that discusses wisdom by relating the tale called "It arose again when the lineage became renowned through means," the eighth, the teaching on how it arose.

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā, that bodhisattva great being, fully asked the question about the ninth of the twelve words concerning what happened before, the one called "It also arose when it was enacted for someone's sake": "O Lord of Secrets, when you yourself, in showing how the supreme secret of the Great Vehicle arose, related the explanation in the form of tales, you set down the words 'It also arose when it was enacted for someone's sake.' In just what ways was that enacted, so that it became renowned?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, it refers to enacting exactly what is unfabricated, just as it is."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, what is it to enact exactly what is unfabricated, just as it is?"

He said, "Lord of Laṅkā, it refers to the nature of reality of phenomena, suchness itself."

He said, "Lord of Secrets, in the nature of reality of all phenomena, in suchness itself, there are no buddhas, there are no sentient beings, there is no male or female, there is no family lineage. It has no form, it cannot be shown, it cannot be indicated, there is no example for it, and there is no activity; it is extremely hard to fathom, and no one is able to realize it. Lord of Secrets, from that in which there is nothing whatsoever, for whose sake do all these many things appear to be enacted: buddhas, sentient beings, male and female, family lineages, various forms, various teachings, indications, different examples, various activities, inferences, and so forth?"

When he had asked this in full, the Lord of Secrets said, "O Lord of Laṅkā, this great and thorough question is excellent! It is most excellent! This meaning, that from what is nothing whatsoever there appears anything at all, is so because that is its very nature. It is simply the appearance of different activities enacted for the sake of three great cities. What are the three cities? They are these: the city of half-appearance, the city of total appearance, and the city of non-appearance. Lord of Laṅkā, since the three cities themselves are the nature of reality, they are called the dharmadhātu, the expanse of phenomena. Why is that? Because what the expanse of phenomena is and what the nature of the three cities is are here one: they are simply the door from which everything arises and the place where it abides.

"Lord of Laṅkā, what appears as three doors for the sake of the three cities is this: the door of dhāraṇī, the door of awareness, and the door of the secret. Lord of Laṅkā, the door of dhāraṇī, too, is made to appear as three for the sake of the three cities, namely: by the condition of thatness itself, calm abiding; by the condition of holding it, insight; and by the condition of entering it, the limit of action. Lord of Laṅkā, for the door of awareness, too, three are made to appear for the sake of the three cities, namely: for the sake of non-appearance, calm abiding; for the sake of appearance, insight; and for the sake of attachment, the limit of action. Lord of Laṅkā, for the door of the secret, too, the making of three appearances for the sake of the three cities appears, namely: for the sake of appearance, calm abiding; for the sake of appearance, insight; and for the sake of attachment, the limit of action.

"Lord of Laṅkā, the making of three buddhas appear for the sake of the three cities is this: for the sake of non-appearance, the unconditioned buddha; for the sake of appearance, the conditioned buddha; and for the sake of attachment, the buddha of form. These are the three.

"Lord of Laṅkā, by the condition of the three cities there are three kinds of sentient beings: by the condition of straying from thatness itself, the formless sentient beings; by the condition of straying from holding it, the sentient beings of form; and by the condition of attachment to entering it, the sentient beings of desire.

"Lord of Laṅkā, by the condition of the three cities, the bodies that are shown as three are these: none, coarse, male and female, and neuter.

"Lord of Laṅkā, by the condition of desire, it appears as though there were fathers and mothers, lines of offspring, and enjoyment of the five objects of desire. Moreover, the Tathāgata himself acts as the mother of the tathāgatas, and the Tathāgata acts as the son of the tathāgatas: he enters the womb, and there too he brings about the cutting of doubts about the supreme secret of suchness, turning back, requesting, turning away requests, granting, and entering into ten millions, as many as ten millions.

"Lord of Laṅkā, for the sake of the three cities there are three teachings: by the condition of thatness itself, the Great Vehicle; by the condition of holding it, the various vehicles; and by the condition of entering it, the lesser vehicle is made to appear.

"Lord of Laṅkā, the three samādhis that appear for the sake of the three cities are these: for the sake of thatness itself, the samādhi of suchness; for the sake of holding it, the all-illuminating samādhi; and for the sake of entering it, the causal samādhi.

"Lord of Laṅkā, the three stūpas that appear for the sake of the three cities are these: the stūpa of the mind, the stūpa of the body, and the stūpa of the speech.

"Lord of Laṅkā, the three maṇḍalas that appear for the sake of the three cities are these: the maṇḍala of the intrinsic nature, the maṇḍala of samādhi, and the maṇḍala of images.

"Lord of Laṅkā, the three mantras that appear for the sake of the three cities are these: secret mantra, vidyā-mantra, and dhāraṇī-mantra.

"Lord of Laṅkā, for the sake of the three cities there are result, condition, and cause.

"Lord of Laṅkā, these and other activities are made to appear fully for the sake of the three cities; they are the full appearance of the activity of the mind."

This concludes the thirty-ninth chapter of the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, the complete perfection of the activity of the mind.

Then again the Lord of Secrets said to the Lord of Laṅkā, "Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore, for the sake of the buddhas, this genuine secret was fully spoken:

The holy state of the buddhas,
Since it is not other than everything,
And since there is nothing at all to adopt or reject,
Is vajra space, without any doubt.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, for the sake of the pratyekabuddhas, this teaching of the vajra of appearance was spoken:

The vajra of speech is without letters;
The vajra of body shows appearance.
Whoever places the mind here
Is fully certain as the secret itself.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that Lord of the Supreme Secret spoke this enactment of the vajra of training for the sake of the śrāvakas:

The vajra of the three doors, ten million in number,
The guarding, the limit of complete purity:
Placing the mind here, on the side of peace,
One is fully certain as the holy one itself.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that holy being spoke this secret vajra of the view for the sake of the yogic sattvas:

Everything is consciousness, appearing as delusion;
There is nothing else; that itself exists as the holy.
Placing whatever consciousness there is here,
One is fully certain as the holy secret.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that holy being spoke this enactment of the vajra of space for the sake of those who fully clear away extremes:

Here, definitively and fully clearing away
The two extremes of existence and nonexistence,
Placing whatever is the nature of mind here,
One is fully certain, without reference, in the Middle Way.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that holy being spoke this making of delusion into vajra for the sake of those possessed of delusion, such as Brahmā:

From the delusion of all the world
The vajra of the mind of awakening arises.
Having placed the mind in the vajra of delusion,
One is fully certain in the vehicle of the great secret.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that holy being spoke this enactment of the vajra of passion for the sake of those possessed of desire, such as Paśupati (Śiva):

All who move and dwell in the world, male and female,
As many as there are, are Paśupati and Umā themselves.
Having placed the mind in the vajra of passion,
One is fully certain in the vehicle of the great secret.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that holy being spoke this enactment of the vajra of killing for the sake of those possessed of aversion, such as Viṣṇu:

All sentient beings, as many as move and dwell,
Are slain by the single great vajra weapon.
Having placed the mind in the vajra of aversion,
One is fully certain in the Vajra Vehicle.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that holy being spoke this enactment of the vajra of turmoil for the sake of those possessed of envy, such as the yakṣas:

Piśācas always act on the desires of others;
Wearing the armor of the all-restraining vajra,
Having placed the mind in the all-agitating vajra,
One is fully certain in the essence of the great secret.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that holy being spoke this enactment of the vajra of slander for the sake of those given to slander, such as the nāgas:

The holy vajra, supreme in dividing,
Divides wrong view from right view.
Having placed the mind in the vajra of slander,
One is fully certain in the vajra secret.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that holy being spoke this enactment of the vajra hard to tame for the sake of those possessed of pride, such as the asuras:

The vajra that, without exception, the hard to tame
Holds down like a great mountain heaped upon them:
Having placed the mind in the vajra of pride,
One is fully certain in the Vajra Vehicle.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that holy being spoke this enactment of the vajra of menace for the sake of the flesh-eaters, such as the rākṣasas:

The vajra devouring, without exception,
Is itself what devours everything.
Having placed the mind in the vajra of menace,
One is fully certain in the Great Vehicle.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that holy being spoke this enactment of the vajra amṛta for the sake of the ḍākinīs and the others, the sorceresses:

The vajra feces and urine are amṛta;
Drinking them is the means for the uncertain.
Having placed the mind in the sorceress vajra,
One is fully certain in the vajra secret.

"Lord of Laṅkā, moreover, that holy being spoke this enactment of the infinite vajra:

In worlds infinite as particles of dust
Are wrong conceptions and discordances numerous as atoms;
By remedies that tame concepts, just as numerous,
They are joined to the expanse of the Vajra Great Vehicle.
Since to the infinite and various beings who move and dwell
Is taught whatever they are inclined toward,
Having placed the mind in the vajra of sentient beings,
They are fully certain as those whose vehicle is the nature of mind.

"Lord of Laṅkā, these are the activities of speech, done for the sake of whoever they are done for."

This concludes the fortieth chapter of the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, the complete perfection of the activity of speech.

Then that Lord of Secrets said to the Lord of Laṅkā, "Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore, the ways in which means are enacted for the sake of others are these: because the domains of beings are different and infinite, the activities too appear as different and infinite. Some domains are ripened by what is pure. Some are ripened by what is impure. Some by abandoning the pure. Some by instructions. Some by idle talk. Some by gentleness. Some by harshness. Some by both. Some by fear. Some by liberation. Some by mire. Some by turmoil. Some by obscuring. Some by fighting. Some by suppressing. Some by burning. Some by raising up. Some by not raising up. Some by harm. Some by not harming. Some by injury. Some by not injuring. Some by censure. Some by praise. Some by regret. Some by not regretting. Some by deceit. Some by boasting. Some by insistence. Some by cajoling. Some by giving. Some by taking. Some by contentment with anything at all. Some by mentioning a name. Some by mentioning a form. Some by showing a form. Some by speaking. Some by incense. Some by ornaments. Some by sound. Some by smell. Some by taste. Some by touch. Some are fully ripened by the Dharma. Some by unpleasant forms. In the same way, some are fully ripened by unpleasant sounds, smells, tastes, and touches. Some by prevailing over them. Some by speaking without interruption. Some by speaking for an instant. Some by saying nothing at all. Some by seeing the holy. Some by the sound of the holy. Some by the sound of a holy spiritual friend. Some by non-conceptuality. Some by conceptuality. Some by suffering. Some by happiness. Some by selflessness. Some by the existence of a self. Some by fierce rites. Some by pacifying. Some by enriching. Some by overpowering. Some by bringing to completion. Some are ripened by the sound that tells of experiencing the suffering of the three poisons. Some by the sound that tells of experiencing happiness. Some by the sound that tells of not experiencing happiness. Some by abandoning the enjoyments of existence and practicing fierce austerity. Some by taking up the enjoyments of existence and conducting themselves in a loose and unrestrained way. Some by the means of prohibition. Some by the means of touching nothing at all. Some by the means of completing the task. Some by giving. Some by pleasant speech. Some by harmony. Some by conduct. Some by giving up everything inner. Some by giving up everything outer. Some by giving up everything. Some are fully ripened by passion for everything. Some by distraction.

Some by settling correctly within. Some by restraining desire. Some by indulging desire. In this way he ripens fully those who act on different inclinations, by showing different forms, sounds, views, means, desires, and aims. Lord of Laṅkā, these should be understood as activities enacted through means."

This concludes the forty-first chapter of the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, the complete perfection of the activity of means.

Then again that Lord of Secrets said to the Lord of Laṅkā, "Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore, the activity of the body for someone's sake is this: it is simply that, just as an emanation of Vajrasattva, set forth in the world, appears in the form of all and so ripens all. That being so, in the doors of the intention of the tathāgatas enacted for someone's sake, in the doors of the speech of the tathāgatas, in the doors of the deeds of the tathāgatas, in the limits of the emanations of the tathāgatas, and in the ocean-like masses of clouds of the means of the tathāgatas, there is nothing at all that can be grasped as 'this' or as 'one.' Why is that? Lord of Laṅkā, it is like this:

Because sentient beings differ in their inclinations,
Infinite people are hard to satisfy;
Since even the Victor could not satisfy them,
He taught infinitely to whatever each was inclined toward.

"Lord of Laṅkā, of the realms of sentient beings, with their endless perceptions, and of their endless sounds, forms, and conduct, there is nothing at all that the holy ones, without moving from suchness, do not know, have not heard, have not seen, or do not explain. Why is that? Because everywhere and in every way they are fully perfected, unmixed, in the very heart of awakening. Therefore, between the nonconceptuality of one who is unmoving in the heart of awakening and a sentient being who dwells in a sesame seed, there is neither far nor near, neither one nor separate: because suchness is one, they abide without duality in a single expanse. Lord of Laṅkā, these are the activities of the body of the tathāgatas, enacted for someone's sake."

This concludes the forty-second chapter of the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, the complete perfection of the activity of the body. From the Way of the Definite Emergence of the Great Vehicle: the Dharma seal that discusses wisdom by relating the tale of how it also arose when it was enacted for someone's sake, the ninth, the teaching on how it arose.

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā, that bodhisattva great being, fully asked the question about the tenth of the twelve words concerning what happened before, the one called "It also arose when the great prophecy was definitively taught": "O Lord of Secrets, when you yourself, in showing how the supreme secret of the Great Vehicle arose, related the explanation in the form of tales, you set down the words 'It also arose when the great prophecy was definitively taught.' In what way was that prophesied, so that it became renowned?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, your question is excellent! It is very excellent! It is most excellent! It is like this. In the expanse of beginningless saṃsāra, in times of eons without beginning or end, buddhas without beginning or end appear as lamps for beings. Through the work of a single means of theirs, those who are to be helped in one direction are ripened; and through the holy means, those who are to be ripened are helped without partiality, in no particular direction. Whoever has pledged to follow after whom, by that very pledge the prophecy that it would arise and appear becomes renowned. For no lamp illumines everything apart from its own domain. Lord of Laṅkā, as for how that prophecy was made, it is like this:

In eons without beginning,
All that has arisen cannot be told;
I shall tell here of one occasion that arose:
You who have donned the armor of the pledge, listen.
In the eon called Fully Arrayed
A billion buddhas arise.
The last of them all to arise,
The Sugata, King of Manifest Emergence,
Prophesied within his assembly:
'In one world or one continent,
In the course of a single eon,
In one land, at one vajra seat,
That two buddhas who complete their deeds
Should arise, I have never truly seen.
If they did, it would be impossible, contrary to the nature of things.
After the Buddha has passed into nirvāṇa,
The wondrous secret essence itself
Will become widely renowned.'
So all the buddhas give the great prophecy.
Just so is this one too:
'When I am no longer present, after my passing,
The very Gathering of the Intentions of All
Will appear for those to be trained at the end of a hundred years.'
So that holy one prophesied.

Further, listen to the great exhortation:
In the eon called Infinite
Thirty-five thousand buddhas arise.
The last of them all to arise,
The buddha Amitābha,
Prophesied within his assembly:
'Just as the earlier buddhas who have passed,
Thirty-five thousand who arose,
Prophesied that the holy one would arise
For the sake of those not ripened by them,
So here too, hold to it fully:
When I have passed and no longer appear,
After a hundred years its meaning will appear.'
The Fierce One gave the blessing and the prophecy.

Further, listen to another great exhortation:
In the eon called Renowned
Forty-six thousand buddhas arose.
The last of them all to arise,
That buddha, Friend of All,
Prophesied within his assembly:
'Just as the holy tathāgatas,
Forty-six thousand who arose,
Prophesied for someone's sake,
After I, the Sugata, have gone,
When fifteen hundred years have passed,
The very holy one, free of dust and stainless,
Who appears as having reached the result,
Here in this supreme and holy field,
A fortunate one who has gained its appearance,
A learned brahmin woman,
Through Tamer of the Harmful himself, will become renowned.'

Further, listen to the great exhortation:
In the eon called Excellent Ornament
Eight hundred million buddhas arose.
The last of them to arise,
The buddha Glorious Excellent Ornament,
Prophesied within his assembly:
'Just as the earlier tathāgatas,
Eight hundred million who arose,
Prophesied the arising of the vehicle,
When this light of mine has set,
At the time of fifty years,
A holy mirror of the Dharma,
By which this is accomplished in a single life,
Here in the world of this continent after I have passed,
A fortunate monk who will arise,
Called Sūryagarbha,
Will let fall the great rain of the teaching
And be renowned as harmonious with all.'

Further, listen to the great exhortation:
In the eon called Endowed with Perfection
A billion buddhas arose.

The buddha Ratnaśrī,
Who arose when all of them had come to an end,
Also prophesied within his assembly:
"Just as those tathāgatas,
The billion who arose,
Gave the holy and definitive prophecy,
After I no longer appear or remain,
In the eighty-second year,
This Gathering of the Secret, the essence of the Great Vehicle,
In the place called the Golden Isle of Sale,
Known as Possessing the Heart of Awakening,
For the fortunate King Protector of Bliss,
Will open the single eye of the Dharma
In accord with karma and compassion,
And all those of noble family will obtain it."

Further, listen to another great exhortation:
In this very Fortunate Eon,
Of the thousand buddhas who arise,
The lamp who arose as the fourth,
The Lord of Sages himself,
Prophesied within his assembly:
"Just as the great prophecy of all was taught,
That the eight hundred forty million
Kings of complete awakening themselves,
Having ripened the regions of beings,
Would make the Vajra Essence of the Great Vehicle itself
Widely renowned,
So, after I no longer appear here,
In the hundred and twelfth year,
A holy essence of the teaching,
Renowned in the divine abode of the Thirty-Three,
To the fortunate one of noble family among humans
In the eastern border region of Jambudvīpa,
To the one called King Ja,
Will be granted first in the form of omens.
On the summit called the Fierce One,
To the companion beings of lesser body,
The Lord of Laṅkā and the others,
Vajrapāṇi will appear."
So the holy one granted the prophecy.
These and others without end,
Without beginning and without end,
Have been renowned in such ways as these.

When the Lord of Secrets had spoken thus, the Lord of Laṅkā and the other beings of lesser body were utterly delighted.

This concludes the forty-third chapter of the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, the prophecy of what would follow. From the Way of the Definite Emergence of the Great Vehicle: the wisdom discussion of the Dharma seal that relates the tale called "It also arose when the great prophecy was definitively taught," the tenth, the teaching on how it arose.

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā, that bodhisattva great being, fully asked the question about the eleventh word concerning what happened before: "O Lord of Secrets, when you yourself, in showing how the supreme secret of the Great Vehicle arises, related the explanation in the form of tales, you set down the words 'It also arose when the stream of karma was fully cut.' In what way was that renowned, and how did it arise?"

The Lord of Secrets said, "Lord of Laṅkā, your question is answered like this:

Among the domains that are to be lifted up,
Those that definitely appear as three
Are the attached to desire, the renunciant,
And the fierce and hard to tame: three to be trained.
The three powers by which they are lifted up,
The continuous wheel, the definitive ultimate,
And the manifest emergence of miracles, these three,
Appear by their nature from the very beginning.
As for the means by which they are lifted up,
Inconceivable, they appear as three;
And what that is, is this:
Nature, force, and the means of remedy,
Which are simply the cutting of the stream of karma.
The continuous wheel, too, appears
From the beginning as the five renowned buddhas.
All those to be trained and all who train
Arise as five, appear as five, are tamed and freed by five:
Producing, sustaining, and supporting;
Cause, condition, and abiding;
All arising in the state of nonconceptuality;
Nonexistent yet inexhaustible, in three aspects.
The expanse, the directly perceived,
That great self itself,
And, in all, no buddha at all.

"Lord of Laṅkā, it is like this:
Those that are awakened in their own expanse,
These that appear as the five great ones,
Are the blessed ones of the stream of the elements,
Buddhas of constant compassion,
Abiding by nature, pervading all.
As beings wish, so they appear as wished,
Renowned as lamps that show appearance and existence.
They are the permanence of the stream of the elements;
Empty of intrinsic nature, they show their deeds.

"Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore,
The buddha of direct perception is
The boundless expanse throughout the expanse of space,
All the things and non-things of the three realms,
The body and the abode of all directions and quarters,
The abode, empty yet present, that gives room to all,
The ground that supports the moving and the unmoving,
The abode where all things are born and arise,
That which joins all in joy:
It abides as the single wheel of an eon.
Kin and dear, producing others, clear by nature,
Holding apart, not turning back, its stream unbroken,
Moistening wilderness and all, moving everywhere:
It abides as the single wheel of an eon.
Ripening all, dispelling darkness, showing all, equalizing,
Burning, softening, making colors appear clearly,
Holding the essences, appearing fully ripened:
It abides as the wheel of a single eon.
Not abiding, unobstructed, it arises with force;
Formless, yet stronger than the power of form,
Light and moving, all-pervading, support of all:
It abides as the single wheel of an eon.

"Lord of Laṅkā, it is like this:
The buddha that is the great self
Is the all-containing, all-hovering Samantabhadra,
The all-bearing, spontaneously dense form of wisdom,
The all-gathering samādhi of equal union,
Insight that clears obscurations, wisdom ablaze,
Unobstructed, undeluded, complete in all that is to be known.

"Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore,
The buddha that is thatness itself is this:
Space is Vairocana;
Earth is the unmoving Vajradhara;
Fire is endowed with Ratnasambhava;
Water, soft and clear, is Amitābha;
Wind, which opens all, without doubt.

"Lord of Laṅkā, it is like this:
That in which there is no buddha here
Is all empty, primordially awakened;
Pervaded by that, unmixed, it is buddhahood itself,
Uncontrived and primordially awakened:
The great elements are the blessed ones.

"Lord of Laṅkā, these
Are the vehicle of the continuous wheel:
Naturally they arise as the continuous wheel,
And their nature abides in all, the stable and the moving.

"Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore,
The vehicle of the definitive ultimate
Definitely appears as three:
The vehicle that leads from the origin of suffering, the awareness of austerity,
And the vehicle of the means of mastery.
As for the leading here, it is this:
The three domains that definitely appear
Appear as lowest, middling, and highest.
Those who have become the lowest domain,
Abandoning extremely dreadful deeds
And taking up the pleasing way of discipline,
Training their own root there,
Appear on the path of individual liberation.
Those who have become the middling domain,
Seeing that all this is dependently arisen,
Selfless, and that not a single phenomenon exists,
Teach the inexpressible, emanated dharmas
And abide at ease through their own power.

Those who have become the highest domain,
Selfless, with love and equanimity, beyond concepts,
Objectless, unborn, and free of partiality,
Easily attuning to the grounds and liberations,
Attain the insubstantial body of essential nature.
Lord of Laṅkā, what more is there?
The vehicle of the awareness of austerity
Is threefold by way of its domains.
What appears to the lowest
Is austerity, vows, and ascetic observances;
Toward equality, unmoving and unborn,
Adopting the manner of reverence and aspiration,
They abide as the Vajradhara of the family.
What appears fully to the middling:
Practicing the karma of purified action,
If they are endowed with yoga and samādhi,
Directly, with an unmoving mind,
They abide as the Vajradhara of the four families.
What definitely appears to the highest:
Mind itself perfects its skill upon the object;
If by awareness one directly sees the object itself,
Having actualized suchness right there,
They abide as the holder of the vajra family.
Lord of Laṅkā, what more is there?
The vehicle of the means of mastery, too,
Appears in three aspects according to domain:
Generation, completion, and total completion.
What appears to the lowest as generation:
From pure skillful means and wisdom,
The dharmas of the five reliances and so forth;
From the five Victors, perfect buddhahood;
The ground of perfection, of great yogic skill.
What appears to the middling as completion:
If, through equality, one sees the meaning of equality,
Equal perfection, knowing the object, space and wisdom,
All-knowing, all-perfect, all-appearing;
The ground of the unchanging, all-pervading nature of reality.
For the highest, total completion is this:
Having realized as mere convention
The meaning called the space of wisdom,
Beyond skill, free of perfecting, beyond the domain of thought;
The ground of the universal mount, in which all is perfected.
Lord of Laṅkā, what more is there?
The vehicle of the manifestation of miracles, too,
Appears in three aspects of domain.
Bad karma of fixation, delusion, and turmoil,
And bad perceptions:
The activity that cuts off their stream and transforms them
Concerns the wheels of the net of the three realms of existence.
In order to cut off the stream of the wheel of karma,
Having turned the sacred wheel,
One will cut through the wheel of the eon.
Why is it that some sentient beings
Fixate through desire and are tormented?
When is it that some sentient beings
Become utterly deluded and benighted?
Through what do some sentient beings
Become extremely wrathful, fierce, and malicious?
Thus, for those in whom the three poisons have ripened,
Whom nothing can benefit,
Even when the characteristic of impermanence has been taught,
In order to cut the karmic stream of bad karma,
The natural miracle becomes manifest.
When wrathful, the five wrathful devourers
Liberate all without remainder and empty them;
Therefore space, the great wrathful devourer,
Empties all by dissolving it:
The supreme liberation into suchness.
By the eon of famine, deprived of the sustenance of existence and peace,
The karmic stream of attachment through fixation is cut.
By the eon of stupor that is endowed with patience,
The karmic stream of mind and mentation is cut.
By the eon of weapons that holds skillful means,
All things whatsoever are cut.
By the eon of fire that is insight,
All afflicted things are burned.
By the eon of water that is calm abiding,
All the stains of affliction are washed away.
By the eon of wind that is unobstructed,
All knowable things are set in motion.
By the eon of space that is wisdom,
All the aging and decay of signs is emptied.
If bad karma is swiftly cut off,
One is swiftly delivered from the bad states.
Among all methods and vehicles,
This is declared to be the last of the last.
In times that have become degenerate,
Because the two kinds of discipline have declined,
The afflictions appear as real things.
As for what is called "passed away and declined,"
That very thing, which has neither passed away nor declined,
Is, at that time, discipline by its own force.
Lord of Laṅkā, in this way,
All of this has never moved at all
From the space of the sacred secret.
From the very space of emptiness alone,
Empty dharmas have merely arisen.
Emptiness, the secret, is suchness;
That is the secret of the Great Vehicle.

When the Lord of Secrets had spoken thus, the Lord of Laṅkā and all the other beings of inferior bodies were left without any doubt that this is definitely the sacred secret.

This concludes the forty-fourth chapter, called "Miracles of the Past," from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

From the ways in which the Great Vehicle definitely arose: the telling of the account "It also arose when the karmic stream was entirely cut off," the discussion of the wisdom of the seal of the Dharma. This is the eleventh teaching on the manner of its arising.

Here the seventh section begins.

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā, that bodhisattva great being, asked a thorough question about what appears as "It also arose when the intention was discussed," the last of the twelve statements about what arose in the past: "O Lord of Secrets, when you yourself were teaching the way the sacred secret of the Great Vehicle arose, at the point where you recounted it in explanation, you set down the words, 'It also arose when the intention was discussed.' Was it discussed in some such way, and so became renowned?"

He replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, what you ask is profound in this way, exceedingly profound; it is difficult in this way, exceedingly difficult; it is not the domain of logicians, it is extremely hard to examine, and it is beyond inference. Since even the buddhas whose continuum is completely purified, and those who dwell on the grounds, are unable to discuss it fully, what need is there to speak of my discussing it fully? It is not the domain of anyone at all."

When he had said this, the Lord of Laṅkā spoke as follows: "O great being, Lord of Secrets, in order to bring benefit and compassion to beings who appear as afflicted, the time has come for you, lion of speech, to discuss unerringly that very suchness: the domain of great persons, the perfect and complete awakening of all dharmas, the enjoyment of the unobstructed nature of reality, the sacred intention of the Great Vehicle of skillful means. Great being, the time has truly come. The time has come to discuss the sacred certainty. Why is that? Because you, great being, have been empowered by empowerment as the lord of the great secret word of all the tathāgatas. So that those who are deluded may have their understanding opened, and so that, with a net of cloud banks of means for guiding them, all who are bewildered may easily be guided, please unfold the body of the sacred intention." Thus urged, the Lord of Secrets spoke as follows:

You who wear the great armor of urging,
Because you are utterly loving toward all,
Since you strive to urge me on this,
You raise the discussion well. Excellent, excellent!
Thus: "Thus I myself" —
Suchness, the definitive meaning —
At the one time when hearing appeared,
I heard as arising, from all that is like space,
As self-appearance itself.
The secret words, "Thus" —
At the one time when they came to be heard,
Taught in keeping with the means of harmonious engagement,
I heard them as a means of guiding.
The reasoning of scripture, "Thus" —
At the one time when I heard it,
Taught by way of applying the hidden intention,
I heard it as a means of leading.
The words that serve a wish, "Thus" —
At the one time when they came to be understood,
What is wished for by those of great desire,
I heard as their being guided by skillful means.
Secret mantra, vajra, great space,
Is not the domain of words,
So it cannot be realized through expression;
For me to discuss it is extremely difficult.
By example, by valid cognition, by reasoning,
Or by the wisdom of inference,
It cannot be realized;
It is like a blind man measuring the height of the sky.
Nevertheless, entrusted with the charge of the Word,
In order to guide by skillful means those wrongly attached,
And because of your sacred pledge in urging me,
Through the power of the armor donned long ago,
For those who have not realized,
Pointing it out with mere words,
As the very means of guiding them in stages
To the pure grounds of liberation,
I shall discuss it as far as is fitting.
Nature, primordially awakened,
Is of two kinds, seen and unseen:
In an inferior situation it is not seen;
It is seen in a sacred situation.
As for the two, seeing and direct hearing,
The ultimate, sacred, direct perception
Belongs to those of intelligence, yogins endowed with the qualities of practice,
Because through hearing they come to see.
The Victor of Ghanavyūha, too,
Since he has become the essence of the dharmakāya,
Because his beings are not ripened,
Shows the form body and speaks in the way of words.
In the measureless times
Of the past, the future, and the present,
Whatever the intention of the Victors,
Listen to how it was discussed.

Lord of Laṅkā, it is like this. If one wishes to enter the intention of the sacred ones, one should enter the treasury. One should leave nature unfabricated. One should grow the tree. One should enter the sacred. One should keep the lamp. One should go to the distinctive. One should enter the object. One should examine the mirror. One should enter in harmony. One should cure illness.

He asked, "Lord of Secrets, how are these ten enumerations of the Dharma to be fully understood?"

He replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, of these ten enumerations of the Dharma, the first, entering the treasury of the sacred ones, is this: by entering through the three doors, one enters the treasury that is inexhaustible in what does not exist."

He asked, "Lord of Secrets, what are the three doors?"

He replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, the three doors are these: the door that guides through conduct, the door of the application of skillful means, and the secret door of certainty. These are the three."

He asked, "Lord of Secrets, by what are those doors opened?"

He replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, know that those doors are opened by three keys. What are the three? They are these: opening, unraveling, and drawing out.

Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore, it is like this:
By the keys of the six branches, the four sūtras, and the three roots,
The treasury of the intention of all the Victors
Is definitely opened; so the Victor has declared.

Lord of Laṅkā, the opening of the door that guides through conduct by the three keys is this: the six branches, the four sūtras, and the three roots.

What, then, are the six branches? The branch of the cause, the branch of application, the branch of the result, the branch of the basis, the branch of retention, and the branch of the characteristic.

What, then, is the branch of the cause? It is this: the branch of arising and the branch of ripening. Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, is the branch of arising? It refers to that which, when fully assembled, gives rise to expression: valid cognition, intelligence, retention, the stūpa, assembly, and certainty. The branch of ripening is the cause of ripening into the sacred. What is it? It is this: the branches of awakening and the branches of the path.

Lord of Laṅkā, the branch of application is application to objects: application to the object of the intellect and application to the object of compassion. Lord of Laṅkā, of these, application to the object of the intellect is applying the root from which intelligence arises to the raising of topics, applying the root by which intelligence is gained to the distinctions of the grounds, applying the root by which intelligence becomes certain to liberation, and applying the root by which intelligence is established to suchness. Lord of Laṅkā, application to the object of compassion, too, is this: application to its cause, application to its result, and application to their nature.

Lord of Laṅkā, the branch of the result, then, is that from which the sacred purpose connected with it arises: the sacred attainment, suchness.

Lord of Laṅkā, the branch of the basis, then, is that which definitively explains the intention. What is it? It is this: that in which dharmas are condensed into three bases. What are the three? The basis of nature, the basis of the root, and the basis of all.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, is the basis of nature? It is this: hearing, penetrating, retaining, and mastering.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the basis of the root? It is this: designation, conduct, birth, abiding, bliss, affliction, purification, variety, agent, action, and ornament.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the basis of all? As it appears in total affliction, it is this: compilation, engagement, person, and dharma. As it appears from complete purity, it is this: designation, effort, abode, bliss, skillful means, knowledge, the basis of knowledge, diligence, absence of pride, habitual tendencies, what is to be actualized, meditation, essence, aspect, the object of focus, investigation, distraction, nondistraction, seeking, qualities, stability, compilation, compilation by topic, realization, what is to be attained, general completion, and thorough reduction.

Lord of Laṅkā, these are called the branch of the basis.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, is the branch of retention? It is this: it should be known solely as taking up, the parental, downfall, nature, the nature of nonexistence, arising, and complete relinquishment. Lord of Laṅkā, these are called the branch of retention.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, is the branch of the characteristic? It is this: the conventional, the ultimate, the object of focus, aspect, nature, result, explanation, obstruction, concordance, disadvantage, and benefit.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, is the conventional? It is this: the person, imputation, and what is taught as action.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, is the ultimate? It is this: separation, stability, and perfection.

The object of focus is what is taught as everything.

Aspect is what is examined as everything: truth, establishment, fault, quality, approach, engagement, reasoning, condensation, and elaboration.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, is truth? It is whatever is suchness.

Establishment is the establishment of the person, of imputation, of oneness, of division, of questions, of establishment, of presentation, of the secret, of explanation, and of answers.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is fault? It is the enumeration of total affliction. What is quality? It is the enumeration of complete purification.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is approach? Meaning, attainment, explanation, the abandoning of extremes, the inconceivable, and intention.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is engagement? Time, assembly, and conditions.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is reasoning? It is this: dependence, the performance of function, proof, and the nature of reality.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, is dependence? It is that which has become the cause and condition for the arising of compounded things and for conventional designation.

What is the performance of function? It is that which has become the cause and condition for attaining some dharma, or for renown, or, once arisen, for performing an action.

What is proof? It is that in which armor has been donned: those things that have become causes and conditions for what is stated and for what is to be ascertained.

Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore, it is like this. Of the pure and the impure, the pure consists of observation, abiding, example, renown, and certainty. Lord of Laṅkā, of these, that all things are impermanent, suffering, empty, and selfless is observation. That all things are momentary, that there is another existence, that actions are impermanent, that there is abiding in action, and that there is abiding in its result is abiding. Applying what is renowned to all, and applying the observation, is example. Their being renowned is renown. That which appears as valid through valid cognition is certainty. Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore, it is certain as valid because it arises, is examined, cuts off, is free, and appears. That is pure through direct perception, inference, and valid cognition.

Lord of Laṅkā, the impure is this: what is similar to something else, what is dissimilar, what is similar to all, what is dissimilar, applying the example of something else, what is not renowned, and what is not certain.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, to observe a valid cognition that lacks the five characteristics of complete purity as similar to something else, and, regarding the characteristics of the compounded, such as impermanence, as they actually are, to observe them otherwise, as permanence, self, and so on, and so, viewing with the four inversions, to observe them as similar to the characteristics of the uncompounded: this is the similar. Being dissimilar to another valid cognition, and dissimilar to another characteristic of an object: this is the dissimilar to something else. To say, "All are the activities of the mind," is the similar to all. Dissimilarity in which, through the dissimilarity of one, they are mutually dissimilar: this is the dissimilar.

Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore, to say, "When a similar example is applied, there is similarity, and so it is uncertain," is the not renowned. To say, "When a dissimilar example is applied, there is similarity, and so it is uncertain," is also the not renowned. Whatever is examined on the basis of what is not renowned is impure. What is impure by nature is uncertain. Lord of Laṅkā, these are proof.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the nature of reality? It is that which is unfabricated in any way.

Lord of Laṅkā, as for condensation and elaboration: through elaboration all things are condensed, and dividing the one through its distinctions, one brings it to completion.

Lord of Laṅkā, the object of focus is apprehending. Aspect is division. Awakening is nature. What is attained by abandoning something is result. What is understood and spoken of is explanation. What is unfavorable to its side is obstruction. What applies diligence on its side is concordance. What inclines under the power of what is discordant is disadvantage. What inclines in concordance is benefit.

Lord of Laṅkā, these are the branch of the characteristic. These six branches are the key of opening. Whatever is the door that guides through conduct, it is opened by this, and the intention is definitively explained just there.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, are the four sūtras? They are these: the sūtras of another intention, the sūtras of hidden meanings, the sūtras of the application of harmonious engagement, and the sūtras of the definitive ultimate.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, the sūtras of another intention are those that intend nature, time, meaning, and object.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, nature is like this. In order to remove the fixations of those who conceive in a certain way of whatever appears as distinct, one intends nature and declares uniformly, "Whatever that is, it is just this."

Lord of Laṅkā, time is like this. In order to arouse enthusiasm in those who are lazy, one intends the infallibility of cause and result and teaches, "By just this much, it will come about swiftly."

Lord of Laṅkā, meaning is like this:
For those who are conceited about something,
In order to dispel their darkness,
Intending that those who have not attained the path of seeing
Do not see suchness itself,
one proclaims, "These cannot be known unless they are explained by countless sacred ones."

Lord of Laṅkā, intending the object is like this. In order that understanding may arise in those who hold that a single aspect is sufficient, one sets out all aspects and speaks in praise of them, and, intending that a single aspect is not sufficient, one disparages the aspect to which they were previously habituated.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, the sūtras of hidden meanings are these: joining, division, overpowering, and deception.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, joining is like this. There are those who are frightened and terrified, those who strive to the point of exhaustion, and those who are fixated and do not engage in anything at all. In order to settle them definitively in the meaning, one intends that whatever has a result exists and that all things lack intrinsic nature, and says, "Everything exists," and, "It is emptiness," in order to instill the corresponding perception.

Lord of Laṅkā, division is like this. In keeping with what each one is inclined toward, one intends three things: explaining the imputed as without real existence, explaining the dependent as dependent origination, and explaining the perfected as unchanging and unerring; and so one teaches the three.

Lord of Laṅkā, overpowering is like this. In order to overpower those of excellent renown by teaching perfect enjoyments, one intends to tame them by becoming their antidote, and teaches what is vast.

Lord of Laṅkā, deception is like this. In order to transform definitively, by hidden means, those whose perceptions are mistaken, one intends the unchanging meaning and teaches a different cause.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, the scriptures of the application of harmonious engagement are these: the scripture that attends to one's own conditions, the scripture that introduces skillful means by blending, the scripture of accordant application, and the scripture of earnest instruction.

Lord of Laṅkā, here, the scripture that attends to one's own conditions is like this: whatever condition suits whomever, one teaches that very thing to that one. The scripture that introduces skillful means by blending is like this: by a means of gathering them in, taking their part and leading them along, one transforms them. Accordant application is like this: by acting in keeping with someone's part, one applies it in this way. The scripture of earnest instruction is like this: whatever someone holds dear, one treasures it for them and establishes it.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, the scriptures of the definitive ultimate are these: doubt, certainty, essence, and the crucial.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, is doubt? It is this: examining why things arose, and recognizing that they are so for that reason. Certainty is like this: finding certainty about whatever has been determined. One's own essence is like this: the basis for stating whatever has been examined. The crucial is like this: whatever someone makes crucial for someone.

Lord of Laṅkā, these are the scriptures of sacred certainty. These four sūtras are the key of unraveling. Whatever is the door that guides through conduct, it is unraveled by this and made clear just there.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, are the three roots? They are these: the root of the intellect, the root of counsel, and the root of skillful means.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, the root of the intellect is like this: in suchness just as it is, it is nonconceptual, free of discursive thought, and vivid.

Lord of Laṅkā, the root of counsel is like this: clear nondelusion regarding what is in every way unmixed with anything.

Lord of Laṅkā, the root of skillful means is like this: purity within total completeness.

Lord of Laṅkā, these are the roots, and these three roots are called the key of opening. Whatever is the door that guides through conduct, it is opened by this and made clear just there.

This concludes the forty-fifth chapter, the chapter explaining the arrangement, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then again the Lord of Laṅkā, that bodhisattva great being, asked the Lord of Secrets a thorough question about the two doors: "O Lord of Secrets, how are the two doors opened, and how is their explanation opened?"

He replied, "Lord of Laṅkā, the two doors, too, are opened and made clear by three keys: the common branches, sūtras, and roots.

The six branches are these: the branch of activity, the branch of skillful means, the branch of application, the branch of conduct, the branch of awareness, and the branch of perfection.

Lord of Laṅkā, these six branches, too, are perfected as one by twelve branches and clearly explained by six branches. What are the twelve? They are these: the branch of the door, the branch of the sacred, the branch of certainty, the branch of intention, the branch of the object of focus, the branch of mantra recitation, the branch of application, the branch of conduct, the branch of attainment, the branch of abiding, the branch of the grounds, and the branch of the view.

Lord of Laṅkā, what are the six? They are these: the branch of the cause, the branch of the root, the branch of connection, the branch of guidance, the branch of certainty, and the branch of application.

Lord of Laṅkā, these should be known as common in every respect. Lord of Laṅkā, these are the branches. Know that those doors are opened by them and clearly explained.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, are the sūtras? They are these: those that explain nonarising, equality, things as they are, and the absence of seeking. Lord of Laṅkā, these are suchness itself, which is the nonarising of anything else, equality, things as they are, the absence of seeking, and what is unmixed.

Lord of Laṅkā, what, then, are the roots? They are these: whatever is suchness, sacred certainty, and complete embrace. Lord of Laṅkā, these are the roots. Know that by them the way those doors truly are is definitively drawn out."

This concludes the forty-sixth chapter, the chapter explaining the compilation, from the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas.

Then again the Lord of Secrets called to the Lord of Laṅkā and said, "Lord of Laṅkā, beyond those, the opening of the secret door of certainty by three keys is especially this:
The secret door of the sacred ones:
By the opening, unraveling, and definitive drawing out
Of branches, sūtras, and roots,
Suchness itself is definitively seen.
Opening is cause, root,
The words of connection, certainty,
And application: these complete the branches,
And they definitely extend to all that arises as speech.
Because the four causes serve the purpose for which something arose,
The essence of the cause is blessed
And definitely appears as an explanation.
The branches of the root are those by which
One goes out to welcome, shows respect, and asks a question,
So that from the occasion a discussion arises.
The branch of connection is of four kinds:
Of expression, the expressed, connection, and intention,
The last two lead on to four.

Guidance here is of ten kinds:
Viewing itself, likewise the opening of doors,
Gathering in general, and leading to abundance,
One's own conditions, hidden intention, the crucial itself,
The unfabricated, the example that points to suchness,
Display, interweaving, and pacifying: ten kinds.
With the ten mirrors and the applying of aspects,
The lamp is applied to search out things in darkness.
By applying the certainties four by four by four,
The twelve all appear as four branches.
Application: the fourth, five, two, and four;
It appears as five branches, and applying these, five;
The sixty-one modes of entering conception, and skillful means,
Are perfected in six branches.

Lord of Laṅkā, the six branches here are these: the branch of the cause, the branch of the root, the branch of connection, the branch of guidance, the branch of certainty, and the branch of application.

Lord of Laṅkā, of these, the branch of the cause is that which has become the cause of arising, namely the six perfect conditions.

Lord of Laṅkā, furthermore, it is like this. At one time, when "Thus did I hear" definitively appeared as suchness itself, the Blessed One, the mind of all the tathāgatas, the one called the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha, free of conventional terms and beyond all designations, the self-nature of great space that pervades all, directly present yet not abiding, impossible to point to, the king of total perfection, was dwelling in the realm called the Great All-Pervading Expanse. It is the place that pervades everything, called the universal mount of perfection; the state of great bliss in the expanse of space; where one laughs in the knowledge that all is awakened; the palace of the Dharma of the universal ground, primordially vast and wondrously renowned, unbroken in its extent; where, in the inexpressible space of the nature of reality, the sun of wisdom has risen beyond measure; without edge or center, everywhere equal in extent. There he dwelt as the great one who abides nowhere at all; attached to nothing, yet the very identity of attachment; doing nothing, yet with all deeds spontaneously accomplished; unborn and unceasing, liberated and pure; not a thing, of the nature of space; unfabricated and unmade, primordially just as it is; not abandoning saṃsāra, primordially spread out and all-pervading; not abandoning the three poisons, the great physician; focusing on nothing at all, yet the source of all focus; where the path of words is cut off, yet the source of all expression; unmixed with anything, totally perfect as one; in every respect the good state, the great crest that never declines; with wisdoms beyond count, and more beyond count, all enveloped without coming together or parting. So he dwelt in the essential nature of insubstantiality.

At one time, when these words of suchness, the secret essence, were heard thus as a means of harmonious engagement, the Blessed One of all the tathāgatas, the Lord of the Supreme Secret, the lord of blessing who has no accepting or rejecting, the array of an inexhaustible treasury from which all desires arise, with a splendor that delights in the sacred enjoyment of all that is great, moving with the great conduct that cuts through the waves, wondrous, was in the realm called Sukhāvatī. It is the place where this very meaning is discussed, the basis where the great assembly is complete, the ground where great skill is perfected.

There, in the Akaniṣṭha palace of the dharmadhātu, was a celestial mansion of various jewels, without edge or center, its pinnacles everywhere equal in extent. It was ornamented with the king of jewels and made beautiful by strands of pearls, hanging clusters, and pendant garlands. On all its rafters, waxing and unwaxing suns and moons hovered, making it bright and forming inlays, and it was studded with stars and brilliant stars like nails. Eight jewel pillars were set up, adorned with suns and moons, and from the jewel capitals, jewel crests and rays of light radiated everywhere. Eight great beams were each draped with cloth. On a canopy of the nature of reality appeared vajra slats. Clouds of miracles gathered thick, the rain of awakening fell, the torment of the afflictions was cleared away, and the sprouts of wisdom grew. From the sound of the nature of reality that resounded came the opening of the discussion of the universal ground, primordially vast and wondrously renowned, free of verbal expression and appearing in the manner of a face and its mirror.

The celestial mansion was Vajra Abhirati. It was ornamented with vajras and made of various jewels, with a floor of gold spread out. Like a mirror, crystal, clear water, or well-refined gold, it was even and level, clear and gleaming, and reflections arose from it. It was soft, springy, smooth, pure, clear, supple, pliant, blazing, radiant, and free of conception. The light of various jewels arose from it naturally, and its rays radiated everywhere, completely dispelling outer and inner darkness. Its crowning pinnacle was a four-spoked wheel that blazed. It was ornamented all over with vajras. Its three doors were all clear, with an unbroken border of light. Sun and moon reliquary ornaments blazed in triple lines, rolled in pairs. It was adorned with steps and porticoes, adorned with four chamfered corners, and made beautiful by courtyards in between. It was adorned with necklaces and great necklaces, held up by archways, beautified with garlands, and adorned with nets and half-nets, and covered with fringes. Blazing vajras and jewels made it radiant all over. From its bells and tinkling bells resounded the Dharma sound of the Great Vehicle, and reflections of the dharmakāya arose. Its base was adorned with perfect ornaments, an unbroken vajra border, and vajra lattices and nets. Palm trees had grown there, hung with golden ornaments and embellished with jeweled inlays, and streams of nectar flowed.

When there was neither outer nor inner, and all had become inner, there was great appearance obstructed nowhere; nothing at all was said, yet it was explained at length; nothing at all was intended; and the king perfect in the mastery of great samādhi was together with a retinue of perfect enjoyment beyond count, and more beyond count, namely: the tathāgata Akṣobhya, king of consciousness, and the rest, all the buddhas of the vajra lineage and class; the tathāgata Great Equanimity, king of feeling, and the rest, all the buddhas of the jewel lineage and class; the tathāgata Signless, king of perception, and the rest, all the buddhas of the lotus lineage and class; the tathāgata Great Being, king of formations, and the rest, all the buddhas of the youth lineage and class; and the tathāgata Imperceptible, king of form, and the rest, all the buddhas of the elephant lineage and class. All of them, purely connected like cloud banks of skillful means and like the space of wisdom, dwelt in perfect enjoyment as principal and retinue.

As a means of guiding, in words of extensive explanation that employ the hidden intention, it is said thus: "Heard at one time." The Blessed Ones, the kings of great miracle who appear as anything whatsoever, were in the buddha-field called Endowed with Inclination, where the abodes are just as they are, in the immeasurable world-system called Treasury of Merit, the place where all activity is completed. There the buddha who guides saṃsāra, the lamp that reveals appearance and existence, the being who clears away what is to be tamed, the noble one who acts for the good of beings, the emanation of compassion skilled in means, the Blessed One of the unbroken stream of arising, emanated as the great self of everlasting compassion, dwelt in a great exposition in the manner of teacher and listeners, together with a retinue beyond telling, beyond even what is beyond telling.

Wishing to show that all things appear in the manner of the two truths, in these words of their own condition, unobstructed, it is said, intending the nature of reality as self-appearance: "Heard at one time." The Blessed One, the secret body, the great bliss, was in the buddha-field renowned as Endowed with Lotuses, which is taught in accordance with this: the place of perfect miracle; the profound distinction of the Uṣṇīṣa Universal Monarch; the delight of the supreme secret, the womb of the Mother, the heart of the lotus, the palace in which all the victors are brought forth; the trace left by the one who has gone for the sake of the goal, the ground that ripens the sentient beings of the three realms of existence; the place where the dependent origination of desire arises, which has become the path of beings. There the uncommon secret body dwelt with a secret retinue beyond telling, beyond even what is beyond telling, namely: the great secret beings Swift Vajra, Great Vajra Rapid, Great Vajra Sharp, Great Vajra Arising, Great Vajra Opening, Great Vajra Great, Great Vajra Vast, Great Vajra Profound, Great Vajra Steadfast, Great Vajra Various, and the rest, the most secret retinue, together with their emerging and entering. They dwelt in the manner of those who cut through doubts, those who have resolved them, and those who press forward to ask.

Thus, for each and every one of the tathāgatas, these distinctions of body appear in this way, and yet there is no appearing as distinct. Free as well from the nature of one and of many, they appear vividly, clearly, brilliantly and distinctly, everywhere and in every way.

At that time, on that occasion, this secret essence of the Great Vehicle of skillful means, the domain of great persons, the meaning of unerring hearing concerning the appearance of the four bodies, is discussed with the heirs of the victors in accordance with suchness, as follows. All the tathāgatas, knowing that all phenomena dawn within the intention of equality, dwelt in the state called unobservable equality of body. They dwelt in the state called unobservable equality of speech. They dwelt in the state called unobservable equality of mind, in which engaging, turning back and turning away are not observed.

Lord of Laṅkā, the two are simply two expressions of whatever occurred. Lord of Laṅkā, these are called the causal branch.

From the Great Sūtra of the Gathering of the Intentions of All Buddhas, the forty-seventh chapter, which teaches the perfect setting.

Lord of Laṅkā, what is the root branch? It refers to the root from which the expression arises, that by which the discourse is set in motion. It is as follows. Then, while those tathāgatas, without moving from the state of the expanse, were as though asleep, from the state of emptiness of the Mother of all buddhas, the Perfection of Wisdom, this sound of great exhortation arose:

Empty of existence and nonexistence, by the power of utmost yoga,
You who from the space-like state have drawn near to the sacred commitment,
Ocean of wisdom, expanse of complete liberation,
Nonabiding and unobservable, arise from the expanse of space!

From that sound, those Blessed Ones, knowing that all phenomena dawn in the heart of awakening, caused a sun vajra to arise from the state of emptiness, just as the heart of the sun rises in a clear sky. Then from that heart of the sun many tens of millions of light rays were sent forth, just as tens of millions of light rays are sent forth from a thousand suns. From those light rays the clouds of compassion, of the mind of awakening, gathered thick, and a rain of play poured down. Thereby they caused the thunder of various plays, the lightning of various plays, the offerings of various plays, the songs of various plays, the dances of various plays, the drums, conches, horns and flutes of various plays, the ornaments of various plays, the delights of various plays, the wisdom of various plays, the primordial wisdom of various plays, the meditative absorption of various plays, the bliss of various plays, the feeling of various plays, the great joy of various plays, and the queen of various plays, the great emptiness, all to enter into suchness.

Then from that state of emptiness of the Mother of all the tathāgatas arose this sound of the nature of reality, of suchness:

Deity of the expanse, heart commitment, who has perfected the two great accumulations,
Body of great play, who brings enjoyment to perfection,
Vajrasattva, fortunate and glorious, you are the lord:
Through the play of skillful means, arise from the expanse of great bliss!

Exhorted by that sound, he arose from that state of great bliss in the form of great miracle. With displays of magic, with swirling, with poise, with majesty, with the great mudrā of dance, with laughter of every kind, with the radiating and gathering of compassion, with the distinctions of qualities, with blazing and radiating light rays, and the rest, he filled all of space with emanations like garlands of lightning. Everywhere and in every way, signs, aspects, existence and nonexistence were completely pervaded by the state of suchness, the essence of secret mantra. Its very nature is secret.

Then again from that great emptiness, the suchness of the Mother of all the tathāgatas, this final sound of exhortation arose:

Body that sports in the illusion of primordial wisdom,
Who has gained mastery over great means and wisdom,
Noble lord, source from which many emanations arise,
Sugata, Sugata, please arise!

Thus exhorted, that Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret, entered into equipoise in the meditative absorption called Vajra Play Suchness, and remained without saying anything at all.

Then, from those sounds of great exhortation that had arisen from the state of the Mother of all the tathāgatas, the Perfection of Wisdom, the very great retinue was amazed and filled with wonder. All of them, with one voice, made supplication from within Vajra Play Suchness with this song:

How wonderful! From the wish-fulfilling supreme body, which is like space,
Performing the vajra dance of all-pervading blessing,
Body expanded in the play of cloud banks of splendor,
You display the miracle of unobstructed vajra play.

How wonderful! From the wish-fulfilling supreme speech, which is like an echo,
Uttering the vajra of melodious blessing,
Speech arrayed with an ocean of irresistible branches,
With a lion's roar you teach the ocean of secret mantra.

How wonderful! From the essence of primordial wisdom, which is like the sun,
Bringing the vajra bliss of blazing blessing,
Mind that is the sphere free of elaboration, nonconceptual,
You display the miracle that ascertains liberation itself.
